# Changelog

## [Unreleased]

- **2026-08-06 — Scene Exporter: *Add New Preset* / *Delete Current Preset* removed, mirroring mayatk.** A preset here is a plain JSON file in the directory *Open Preset Directory* (`b007`) opens, so adding and deleting is done there; `b003`/`b004` are gone from the option box and from the slots. The engine keeps the programmatic equivalents — `SceneExporter.save_fbx_preset` / `delete_fbx_preset` are the `PresetStore` surface the tests drive and the built-in/user two-tier rules live on, not UI glue — so only the buttons went away.

- **2026-08-06 — Scene Exporter: *Max Size (MB)* is a spin box, mirroring mayatk.** `check_texture_file_size` moves from `QLineEdit` (free text, `value_method: "text"`) to a uitk `SpinBox` bounded at `[0, 4096]` handing the check an int, with `setCustomDisplayValues({0: "OFF"})` so 0 is a labelled OFF position instead of an empty field. The check still parses numeric strings for templates saved before the change.

- **2026-08-06 - Scene Exporter: row captions, mirroring mayatk.** The same six definitions (`export_visible_objects`, `set_linear_unit`, `ignore_groups`, `version`, `check_framerate`, `check_texture_file_size`) declare `set_row_label`; `check_texture_file_size` and `ignore_groups` ship with `setText` defaults, so their placeholders never rendered and the rows read as a bare `16` / `temp`. Rendered by uitk's `WidgetComboBox` row caption; no slot change (`**params` already reaches `set_attributes`). The parity check mirrors mayatk's: any captionless `widget_type` without a `set_row_label` fails.

- **2026-08-06 - Mirrors mayatk's shared-RPC-core change.** `marmoset_rpc` / `substance_rpc` now carry a staged `_rpc_core.py` (verbatim from `pythontk.net_utils.rpc.plugin_core`) instead of their own `server.py` + `registry.py` + `main_thread.py`, and contribute only their `RpcPlugin` config and ops; `substance_rpc` gains `system.describe` as a side effect. Also newly drift-guarded against mayatk by `extapps/test/test_vendor_sync.py`: the RizomUV `scripts/*.lua` presets (pure target-language data - the UV algorithms themselves, where a fix landing on one DCC and not the other is silent) and the Unity bridge's `parameters.py` + `.ui`. The guard now compares *semantic* twins - docstrings, `--` comment blocks and the host vocabulary (Maya/Blender, mayatk/btk, cmds/bpy) fold to neutral tokens before the assert - which is what let two genuinely-shared halves be guarded at all instead of exempted case by case. `test/test_bridge.py` renamed to `test_edit_bridge.py` (it tests the mesh bridge op, not a hand-off bridge).

- **2026-08-06 — `ungroup_objects` crashed when the same group appeared twice in `objects`.** The loop called `bpy.data.objects.remove(grp, do_unlink=True)` with no already-removed guard, so a repeated group hit a freed datablock: `ReferenceError: StructRNA of type Object has been removed`, which both propagated to the caller and skipped every later group in the list. The Maya twin guards this deliberately (`if not cmds.objExists(grp): continue  # same group passed in twice`); bpy frees the pointer rather than leaving a name to test, so the fix dedupes up front instead (`dict.fromkeys`, which keeps the caller's order). Not reachable from the `m_ungroup()` hotkey — a view-layer selection cannot contain duplicates — so this was an unintended asymmetry on the public `btk.EditUtils.ungroup_objects(objects)` entry point rather than a live regression. Pinned in `test_edit_utils.py` (fails on the old code with the exact ReferenceError).

- **2026-08-06 — `blenderpy-package-manager.bat`: self-sufficient single-file download + raw-endpoint-safe line endings.** Twin of the mayatk change: on a missing shared `package-manager.bat` (single-file download — the wheel ships it alongside, a lone download doesn't) the wrapper now curls the blendertk mirror and normalizes its line endings before handing off; `.gitattributes` flips `*.bat` from `text eol=crlf` (LF in the blob, so raw.githubusercontent served cmd-mangling LF-only bats) to `-text` with the files renormalized to CRLF byte-verbatim.
- **2026-08-06 — WebXR preview: the scene sidecar now honours `EMBED_TEXTURES=False`.** The panel's *Include Textures* toggle promises a fast, flat-material push, but the sidecar walked the texture manifest and embedded base-colour/emissive maps as data URIs regardless — smuggling textures back in after the FBX skipped them, and paying the shader-graph walk the toggle exists to avoid. `_scene_sidecar` now takes `include_textures` (fed from the request's `EMBED_TEXTURES`) and carries constants only when it is off; the manifest walk is skipped entirely. Verified in a fresh headless Blender 5.1 (Principled BSDF with an emission map): with textures the emissive map rides the sidecar, without it only the constant base colour travels. Mirror of the mayatk change.

- **2026-08-06 — `WebXrPreview`: push the selection to a live browser / WebXR preview (mirror of mayatk).** `btk.WebXrPreview` ↔ `mtk.WebXrPreview`, same public shape (`push` / `url` / `stop`) and the same behavior, so the tentacle Rendering slot stays branch-free. Genuinely the same code path, not a parallel port: both are `pythontk.HandoffBridge` subclasses over the shared `ptk.PreviewDeliverer`, so the FBX → GLB → publish half is one implementation and only the selection read differs — `BlenderExportMixin` supplies that, exactly as it does for the Maya and Unity bridges. The module never imports `bpy` (the mixin defers it), so the package surface still resolves without a running Blender — verified by resolving `btk.WebXrPreview` under the plain workspace venv.

  Export defaults live on `PreviewBridge` and map onto options `BlenderExportMixin._fbx_options` already reads, so no new export surface was needed. Exposed in tentacle's Rendering panel as `tb002`, objectNames shared with the Maya fork; the parity sweep passes with no new delta.

  **The scene sidecar mirrors mayatk's, and measuring Blender is what justified the whole approach.** Unlike Maya's, Blender's FBX exporter *does* write emissive — but it arrives wrong: an authored `(0, 1, 0.5)` at strength 3 lands as `[0, 1, 1]`, which is exactly `color × strength` clamped to LDR. The FBX emissive channel has no strength companion, so intensity cannot survive it in either DCC; that measurement is why the fix is a sidecar carried around the FBX rather than an export flag. With it on, the GLB carries the authored `[0, 1, 0.5]` plus `KHR_materials_emissive_strength: 3.0`; with it off, the mistranslated `[0, 1, 1]` — which is the toggle earning its place as a probe.

  The material list and texture manifest are resolved **once** in `_scene_sidecar` and shared by both readers, mirroring mayatk — `MatManifest.build` walks every assigned node tree, and doing it twice per push is wasted work on an operation meant to feel immediate.

  A `base_color` section rides alongside, mirroring mayatk's for the same reason — a preview whose surfaces are all flat white leaves emissive nothing to read against. Only the *constant* is carried: a texture-driven base colour already travels through the FBX as a real image, so re-embedding it would only bloat the payload.

  Emission is read from the Principled BSDF's `Emission Color` / `Emission Strength`, with `MatManifest` supplying the connected texture (it already owns the socket rename across Blender versions). The same trap as the Maya twin, caught the same way: `MatManifest` **cannot enumerate** here because it drops materials whose slot dict is empty, so a colour-only emissive was invisible until enumeration moved to `MatUtils.get_mats`. Strength defaults to 0 on a fresh Principled BSDF, so it is multiplied into the colour rather than read past — otherwise a material that renders black reports as emissive.

- **2026-08-06 — `m_ungroup` — the missing inverse of `m_group` (mirror of mayatk).** New `EditUtils.ungroup_objects` + the macro over it, so `Ctrl+Shift+G` has something to bind to. Each selected group Empty hands its children up one level (to its own parent, or the world), then is removed. The keep-transform reparent is `NodeUtils.reparent`, not a hand-rolled `parent` + `matrix_world` loop — that primitive already exists for exactly this and settles the depsgraph between levels, which the nested case needs. Only `EMPTY` objects count as groups — the Blender reading of Maya's "transform with no shape" — so a mesh or armature in the selection is skipped, not deleted. Deepest-first ordering hands an inner group's children to the outer group so they are freed again on its turn, deduped so a leaf under two levels is returned once. **The freed children are left selected**: Maya's `ungroup` does that implicitly and the Maya suite now asserts it, so the mirror reproduces it explicitly rather than dropping the user on an empty selection after a hotkey; objects outside the active view layer are skipped, since `select_set` raises on them. Pure data API (no `bpy.ops`), so it runs headless. +6 checks (Empty removed, children unparented, world position held at x=0 / x=4 after the group's own offset, children left selected, non-Empty selection skipped, nested outer+inner freeing the leaf exactly once at its original position); test_macros 78/78, test_edit_utils and the smoke suite green in a fresh Blender 5.1.2. `ctl+sht+g` bound in the `m3trik` macro preset.

- **2026-08-05 — Naming: the Rename field's wildcard grammar was half-implemented and its tooltip advertised a feature that did not exist (mirror of mayatk).** Inherited whole, since both packages' `Naming.rename` delegates the pattern work to pythontk's `find_str_and_format` — root cause and measurements are in that entry. Everything past a single plain Find term silently did nothing: a piped Find like `*_L|*_R` collapsed to one literal matching no name, so `*chars*` and strip became no-ops and `*chars` / `chars*` degraded to a blind append, which reads as a rename succeeding rather than a suffix replacement being skipped. Regex selected the right objects and then formatted them wrongly or not at all, and **Ignore Case** never reached the substitution. The tooltip's "Combine terms with `|` for multiple renaming criteria" was never implemented in any form — that documented example renamed every matched object to the literal pattern text.

  Piped Rename terms now pair positionally with Find's (`*_L|*_R` → `*_lt|*_rt` renames each side in one pass; a single term applies to every Find term), regex capture groups are available in Rename as `\1` / `\2` / `\g<name>`, and `**` alone is the no-op it reads as. Tooltips rewritten around the mental model previously left implicit — **the asterisk marks the part of the existing name that is kept** — and they now state the append fallback for an empty or absent Find. Text is identical to mayatk's, per the panel-surface mirror. +4 checks (paired terms, per-term filter text, regex backref, regex replace-suffix cutting at the match); test_naming 31/31 in a fresh Blender 5.1.2.

- **2026-08-05 — RizomUV bridge: Pre-scale / Mix Scale tooltips now state what the knobs measurably do (mirror of mayatk).** Reported and measured on the Maya side ("Pre-scale gives the same result no matter what value I choose"); the twin registry carries the identical `SCALING_MODE` / `SCALING_MIX` specs, so it told users the same misleading story. `Scaling.Mode` is honoured by RizomUV — on a 4:1 two-cube probe with identical incoming UV islands, `Mode=0` holds the packed island-area ratio at 1.000 while `Mode=1/2` take it to 16.000 — but it is unobservable at the shipped defaults, because modes 1 and 2 differ only by a global factor that the default `LayoutScalingMode=2` best-fit renormalizes away, and because all three modes agree once the incoming UVs already have consistent texel density (the normal state after any unwrap). `Scaling.Mix` measured a byte-identical no-op on 2020.1. Both tooltips now say so; the structural cleanup (dropping the key, collapsing the choices) stays in the workspace backlog pending a ≥ 2022 re-probe. Construction test 36 green.

- **2026-08-05 — RizomUV bridge: `ZomPack Resolution` is no longer version-stripped on 2020.1 (mirror of mayatk).** Diagnosed and measured on the Maya side, where the symptom was reported ("pack has to be sent twice before it fills the UV space", worst with instances); the twin carried the identical `MIN_VERSIONS` gate, so it inherited the same defect. That gate was a documented *guess*, not a probed crash, and `Resolution` — distinct from the genuinely crashing `MapResolution` — is safe on 2020.1. Sending it is what makes a single send converge: 4 DAG instances went `0.5766 → 0.6655` tile coverage across two sends and now reach **0.6777 on send 1 with a second send changing nothing**; a mixed instances+unique scene gains +11.3%. Trade-off recorded in the gate comment: an all-unique scene is slightly worse at 1024. `PACK_MAX_MUTATIONS` and `PACK_ROTATE_ENABLE` stay gated — measured as no help for the stacked/instances case at any value, with `MaxMutations` costing 8× runtime where it helps at all.

  Fixing it exposed a latent fragility in this repo's own test: `test_rizom_construction` asserted the gated fields were absent with a **raw substring scan over the whole script**, so the new explanatory comment in the shared `pack_block.lua` — which names `MaxMutations` in prose while explaining why it stays gated — read as code and failed the check. The field-presence assertions now scan code lines only, via a `code_lines()` helper alongside the `zom_lines()` one that already existed for exactly this hazard (wrapper.lua's header mentioning `UseUVSetNames`). +2 checks (`Rotate.Enable` kept above the gate; `Resolution` surviving below it) — 36/36, rizom_roundtrip 1/1, uv_utils 70, uv_shells 25, uv_set_cleanup 12.

- **2026-08-05 — Reference Manager: renaming the *open* .blend stranded the session on the old filename (mirror of mayatk).** `rename_scene_file` only touched disk, so `bpy.data.filepath` kept pointing at a name that no longer existed: edits made since the last save had nowhere to go, and the next save silently resurrected the old file beside the renamed one. Renaming the open file is now save-then-reopen — flushed to the old `.blend` first (saving after the rename would just re-create the old name), then re-opened on the new path; a save that fails aborts the rename rather than stranding the edits. A foreign row can't trip it: the comparison is against `bpy.data.filepath`, which is always a `.blend`. +4 checks (86/86).

- **2026-08-05 — Substance bridge: `ensure_rpc_plugin` now gates on content, not presence (mirror of mayatk's fix).** A `substance_rpc` install predating the setup ops was never refreshed — the presence check passed and, without Developer Mode, the install is a `copytree` snapshot rather than a symlink — so `project.set_resolution`, `bake.set_high_poly` and `textures.apply_mesh_maps` were never registered and the panel's Map Resolution silently lost to Painter's 1024 default. Gated on the new `Installer.is_current()` (over pythontk's `PluginInstaller.is_plugin_current`); the log distinguishes *Installed* from *Refreshed*.
- **2026-08-05 — Hand-typed text fields grew the clear button that only Naming and Channels had (mirror of mayatk).** `option_box.clear_option` is opt-in per field, so identical-looking fields behaved differently. The rule applied: a clear button belongs on a field the user types into, where empty is a state worth reaching. Added to Reference Manager's filter, Lightmap Baker's name affix, Image→Plane's material suffix, Emissive Groups' group name, Tube/Wheel Rig's rig name, and Blendshape Animator's name prefix. Not added to browse-driven or auto-populated paths (Reference Manager root dir, Scene Exporter output dir, Workspace Editor project root, Image Tracer path, Shot Manifest CSV path), nor to Wheel Rig's numeric height or Channels' read-only target field.

- **2026-08-05 — A commit with the preview OFF skipped `prepare_operation` and the OBJECT-mode guard entirely.** Found while mirroring mayatk's new bypass path onto this side. `commit()`'s no-preview branch read the selection and went straight to `perform_operation`, so the one-shot precondition `enable()` runs never fired — for Mirror, its only user, that hook is what breaks linked-data sharing, and its own docstring is explicit that mirroring geometry "can never leave data shared (it would rewrite every linked duplicate)". Pressing Create without previewing therefore mirrored straight onto a shared datablock. A *failing* precondition was worse than skipped: the guard is what aborts an unsafe run, and with it bypassed the operation went ahead anyway (proved against the old code — `boom.ran == 1` where the previewed path leaves it 0). The same branch also never called `_ensure_object_mode()`, so the object-level pipeline could run from a live edit session. Both gates now live in one `_prepared_selection()` — OBJECT mode → selection → precondition, reporting through `message_func` and returning None to abort — shared by `enable()` and `commit()`, so the two entry points can no longer drift apart the way they had. +4 checks; two fail against the old code with the reported symptoms.

- **2026-08-05 — `Preview` gains `require_preview` (mirror of mayatk).** This Preview already committed without a preview — a click with the toggle off runs the operation once on the live selection — but the commit button was hard-enabled at construction with no way back, so a panel that must not commit blind had no lever. The state now routes through `_sync_commit_enabled`, asserted at construction and re-asserted on enable/disable/commit, and `require_preview=True` gates both halves: the button stays disabled until the preview is on, and `commit()` with no preview running is a no-op. Default is unchanged (button live, blind commit allowed). Same parameter name and behavior as mayatk's, where this cycle's change moved the Maya side onto the same default. +5 checks. Headless suite 46/46; bevel 14/14, bridge 15/15.

- **2026-08-05 — Color ID: Reset wiped every channel instead of the checked ones (mirror of mayatk).** `ColorIdSlots.b000` called `ColorId.reset_colors(objects)` with no flags, so all five `reset_*` parameters fell back to their `True` defaults — Set Color and Select By Color both routed the checkbox states through `_channels()`, Reset alone ignored them. Clearing a wireframe tint therefore also stripped the object's ID material, its color attributes, its outliner stamp and its ID collection. Reset now takes the same `_channels()` dict as its siblings (`wireframe` → `reset_object`, the channel's datum being `obj.color`), and all four buttons route it through a `_channels_or_warn()` guard that reports rather than acting on an empty channel set — with nothing checked, **Select By Color used to clear the user's selection** (a zero-check query matches nothing, and it selects its result). +8 checks in the headless suite driving the real slot with stub checkboxes (72/72); six fail against the old code with the reported symptoms. See mayatk's CHANGELOG — the Maya side carried the same defect plus two more that have no Blender analogue (its material lookup queried the transform rather than the shape; its vertex reset filtered out shapes).

- **2026-08-04 — Scene exporter: texture-size gate is now a QLineEdit (mirror of mayatk).** `check_texture_file_size` swaps the fixed ComboBox steps for a free-form MB field; the limit arrives as the widget's text, so the check gains the same coercion mayatk has (`None`/`0`/`""`/`"OFF"` disables, non-numeric warns and skips; oversized report formats the limit with `:g`). +2 checks in the headless suite (60/60). The mayatk change rides a larger fix — FBX Embed Media resolves relative paths against the process CWD — that has no Blender analogue (Blender's exporter resolves `//` paths against the .blend itself).

- **2026-08-04 — Texture Path Editor: "Make Paths Absolute" header command.** Mirror of the new mayatk action (inverse of Normalize Paths): rewrites (selected, or all) `//`-relative paths to absolute via the engine's `normalize_texture_paths("absolute")` mode, which existed but was never exposed in the panel. The slot guards on an unsaved .blend before running: `//` resolves against the .blend itself, so with no saved file Blender falls back to the process CWD and would bake a bogus absolute path into the datablock (the Maya twin has the same guard on an unset workspace). Headless test gains an absolute→relative round-trip in the saved-workspace layout (31/31); parity sweep clean; live-GUI pass owed.

- **2026-08-04 — Lightmap Baker's affix tooltip lost the `<object>` placeholder it exists to explain.** Mirrors the mayatk fix (see its CHANGELOG): the `.ui` carried `&amp;lt;object&amp;gt;`, which XML-decodes to a literal `<object>` that Qt's rich-text parsing then swallowed as an unknown tag — the line rendered "is treated as a suffix → `_Lightmap`". Now double-escaped so it reaches Qt as literal text, with the collapsed paragraph break restored as `<br/><br/>`. Verified through `QTextDocument`; `check_tooltips.py` is clean.

- **2026-08-04 — Marmoset mirrors re-synced: `_source` suffix default + the new `SUFFIX_INCLUDE_CHILDREN` row.** The vendored slices (`template_params`, `parameters`, `_toolbag_helpers`, `templates/bake.py`) pick up `_source` as the default Source Suffix (`_target` added to the target list) and the new parameter that gates whether a suffixed **parent** classifies every mesh under it — group-root tagging, previously unconditional and undiscoverable. The Blender DCC-half follows: `_classify_blender_chain` and `build_bake_pairs_manifest` take `include_children`, and `_produce` resolves the pairing keys through `template_params.DEFAULTS` rather than a second hardcoded `"_high"`. Vendor-sync DCC-pair gates green. Note the suffix rows do NOT grey out here the way mayatk's now do when a Bake Source set exists — blendertk has no `BakeSourceSet` yet, so there is nothing to supersede them (ledgered in the workspace backlog with the rest of the bake_sets mirror).

- **2026-08-04 — Marmoset/substance bridge mirrors re-synced with mayatk's bake-transfer fixes.** The DCC-agnostic engine slices (marmoset `_marmoset_engine` / `_toolbag_helpers` / `template_params` / `parameters` / `templates/bake.py`; substance `parameters` + three templates) picked up the Toolbag module-scalar neutralization (imports leave `Roughness=0.3`, `Metalness=0.0` multiplying any map wired later), the derived bake padding/bit-depth (`TemplateParams.derive_bake_values` — padding from `MathUtils.calculate_uv_padding`, bits from `ptk.OutputTemplates`), and the new `BAKE_SOURCE_SET` action param row (the shared surface renamed high/low -> source/target); the substance panel's header High Poly items moved to that row and its handlers renamed to match (`set_bake_source_from_selection` etc.). The marmoset DCC-half's pairs-sidecar classifier now emits 'source'/'target' to match what the synced template reads -- its old 'high'/'low' values would have been silently ignored. `extapps/test/test_vendor_sync.py` DCC-pair gates green. Note: the marmoset panel's Bake Source row renders disabled until blendertk grows the shared `bake_sets` BakeSourceSet + slot handlers (ledgered in the workspace backlog, along with the `_high` vs `_source` companion-suffix divergence).

- **2026-08-04 — An ambiguous `Normal` outranked an explicitly tagged one when a set carried both.** `create_pbr_material` selected the normal to wire with `next(k for k in ("Normal", "Normal_OpenGL", "Normal_DirectX") if k in by_type)` — generic first. So a set holding both `rock_Normal.png` and `rock_Normal_OpenGL.png` shadowed the labeled map with the unknown-convention one and then green-flipped it or not according to the panel combo, which is the exact opposite of the rule the very next lines enforce ("an explicit tag in the FILENAME outranks the caller's setting"). Selection now goes through the shared `ptk.MapRegistry.select_normal_type` (`Normal_OpenGL, Normal_DirectX, Normal`) rather than a local tuple — mayatk's GameShader picks its normal from the same call, and since the two packages cannot import each other a hardcoded copy on each side was exactly what let them drift. `resolve_pbr_plan` additionally runs `ptk.MapFactory.resolve_normal_maps` beside its existing `filter_redundant_maps` call, so a set carrying two normal types no longer wires the Principled Normal input twice (the three types have no `replaces` relationship, so redundancy filtering never collapsed them). `target_format` is left None here on purpose: the graph flips green itself, so nothing needs converting on disk — mayatk, whose bump2d cannot, asks the same call for a converted file. `_SHADOWED_BY`'s Bump/Height entries read `MapRegistry.NORMAL_TYPES` for the same anti-drift reason. `test_mat_anim_utils.py` +2. (The green-flip behaviour itself was already correct here — it is what the mayatk fix in this cycle brings the Maya side up to.)

- **2026-08-04 — Substance bridge: per-texture-set mesh maps and packed-map unpacking, mirroring mayatk.** Same three changes — `--mesh-map` narrowed to genuine mesh maps (a base-color map has no slot to land in), the manifest's new `mesh_maps` section driving the plugin's `textures.apply_mesh_maps` op so each material's AO/normal reaches *its own* texture set, and `PAINTER_UNPACK_MAPS` (default on) splitting ORM / MRAO / MSAO / MetallicSmoothness / AlbedoTransparency into the components Painter can actually read. See mayatk's CHANGELOG for the reasoning and measurements. All six shared helpers (`mesh_map_files`, `_mesh_map_assignments`, `_unpack_packed_map`, `_apply_prefix`, `_project_setup_ops`, `high_poly_path_for`) are AST-identical across the two packages — they route through `pythontk.MapFactory`, which both already depend on, so the split is naming-only. Panel parity sweep clean; API registries regenerated.

- **2026-08-04 — Substance bridge: the high-poly bake source and Map Resolution knobs, mirroring mayatk.** Same two panel parameters (**Export High Poly** → a companion `<name>_high.fbx` wired into Painter's baking options; **Map Resolution**, default 4096), the same vendored templates / `parameters.py` / `substance_rpc` plugin ops (`project.set_resolution`, `bake.set_high_poly`, applied immediately on an open project and otherwise replayed on `ProjectEditionEntered`), and the same rule that the export **never modifies the scene** — hidden geometry rides into FBX verbatim, so no unhide/re-hide pass exists to leave anything behind. See mayatk's CHANGELOG for the measurements behind that.

  Direction-specific: membership is a **stamped Collection** (`substanceBridge_highPoly`, custom property `btk_substance_high_poly`) rather than an `objectSet`, looked up by the stamp and never by name — the `ColorId` idiom, so a user's own collection that happens to share the name is never adopted. Members are *added* to it and left in their existing collections: the set is a tag, not a re-parent. Both membership-dropping paths — `clear()` and the redefine that replaces the set wholesale — route through `_rehome_if_last`, which links an object to the scene root when ours was its only collection; a zero-collection object is orphaned data, gone from the view layer and collected on the next save/load. **Select High Poly reports rather than forces**: `select_set` raises on an object outside the active view layer and silently refuses one with `hide_select` on, and unhiding geometry behind the user's back to satisfy a *select* action would be the one thing this feature promises not to do — the unreachable ones are counted in the log and still export. Panel parity sweep clean; API registries regenerated on both sides.

- **2026-08-03 — `EnvUtils.export_scene_as_obj()` — the twin of mayatk's new OBJ exporter.** Same name and same parameter meanings on both sides (`selection_only` / `materials` / `smoothing` / `normals` / `groups` → `export_selected_objects` / `export_materials` / `export_smooth_groups` / `export_normals` / `export_object_groups`), so tentacle's Export Scene format combo dispatches to either engine with no branch. Wrapped in `window_context_override` for the same reason `FbxUtils.export` is: the bundled exporter calls `context.window.cursor_set`, which is an AttributeError when window is None (the Qt-pump state). Live-verified in headless Blender 5.1: whole scene → group records, normals, UVs and the `.mtl` sidecar; `selection_only` → the cube alone (8 verts, not 490); `materials=False` → no sidecar. New `test_env_utils.py` (8 checks) mirrors the mayatk block — the shared parameter contract only holds if both are exercised the same way.

- **2026-08-03 — Translated materials stop arriving in Blender wearing an `_fbxsafe` marker, and the send defaults to the game shader.** FBX carries only the classic Lambert/Phong model, so pulling a Maya scene swaps each modern shader for an equivalent phong — and that phong was NAMED `<mat>_fbxsafe`. That name is what rides in the FBX, so it became the Blender material's name, was saved into the `.blend`, and rode back to Maya on the next send (`REF_Agilent_E4419B_fbxsafe1` — live production report). Only the manifest-driven rebuild ever undid it, so an untextured or unclassifiable material kept the marker forever. The phong now TAKES OVER the source material's name (the source is renamed aside in the throwaway conversion scene), which makes the FBX itself truthful — even a hand-rolled import gets real names, with no dependence on a successful rebuild. The SG-sharing memo is keyed by the post-rename node name, since that is what the remaining shading groups report; keying by the old spelling would re-translate the material per shading group and reintroduce the duplicate explosion. `_claim_material_name` mirrors mayatk's fix on the import side: a rebuilt material takes its name back once the FBX one it clashed with is purged (`M_x.001` → `M_x`), and never steals a name still in use. **Rebuild Shader now defaults to Stingray PBS** — these hand-offs feed a game engine, and it is the only shader family that declares its texture slots, so its maps survive the round trip instead of being re-guessed from filenames. The spec itself moved to `uitk.bridge.Parameters.shader_type_spec()` (the pull direction runs the same rebuild and takes the same choice as an `import_scene` keyword). Template-hygiene checks +2, name-reclaim checks +2 against real Blender datablock naming, plus an exact-name assertion in `test/send_live_e2e.py`.

- **2026-08-03 — Send to Maya can now rebuild materials as OpenPBR Surface or Stingray PBS, not just Standard Surface.** New **Rebuild Shader** option on the Maya-bridge panel (`SHADER_TYPE`), carried through the rendered template into `mtk.BlenderSceneImport._apply_texture_manifest` and on to `GameShader`, which has supported all three shaders all along — the bridge was the only thing pinning it to one. The choice list uses GameShader's own vocabulary (`standard_surface` / `open_pbr` / `stingray`) so nothing has to translate between two spellings, and the default value lives in the Qt-free `_maya_bridge.DEFAULTS` beside the others (the specs read it), keeping `save_as` usable under headless Blender where Qt cannot be imported. Both send templates consume it — the interactive `import` and the `save_as` `_save_scene` — so an artist gets the same shader whether they send to a live Maya or write a `.ma`. Standard Surface was the default at first (renders anywhere, no plugin); the entry above moved it to Stingray PBS. A Maya that cannot build the requested type still falls back to Standard Surface with a named warning rather than losing the material (see mayatk's CHANGELOG). Live-verified end to end on Maya 2025 via `test/send_live_e2e.py`, which gained two legs asserting the resulting `.ma` really carries an `openPBRSurface` / `StingrayPBS` shader (and tolerates the documented fallback on installs lacking either).

- **2026-08-03 — The manifest's slot trace was socket-blind, so the canonical cutout material lost BOTH its color and its alpha.** `MayaBridge._material_slots` walked forward from each image NODE, lumping every output socket together. Blender's standard alpha setup wires one image's `Color` into Base Color **and** its `Alpha` into the Principled `Alpha` — read node-wide that is two channels, which is indistinguishable from a packed ORM, so the image was rejected as ambiguous and *neither* channel reached Maya. The variant where only the alpha is traceable was worse: it recorded a plain COLOR file as the material's opacity map, which is what fed the Maya-side mis-wiring (see mayatk's CHANGELOG). An image's `Color` and `Alpha` outputs are different DATA, so the trace now seeds per OUTPUT SOCKET (interior hops stay node-keyed — a converter's outputs are one signal). The cutout material now records `{baseColor, opacity}` off the one file, and Maya binds `outColor → baseColor` + `outAlpha → opacity` from it exactly as `ShaderAttributeMap` declares. The packed-map rejection is unweakened: ambiguity is now measured per socket, so an ORM's `Color` reaching Roughness + Metallic through a Separate Color still records nothing and leaves the filename taxonomy in charge. `test_maya_bridge.py` +2 (Color+Alpha off one image resolves both; a packed map's Color socket still records nothing); live cutout leg added to `test/send_live_e2e.py`.

- **2026-08-03 — USD pull: instance replay GUARANTEED-OR-FAIL (v2 PATH sidecar), and the `mtl` materials-scope Empty no longer lands in the outliner.** Mirror of mayatk's entry — the four USD legs shared the silent-flattening failure class; see mayatk's CHANGELOG for the full v2 contract (always-written sidecar, artifact withheld on sidecar failure, strict member accounting, loud bake). Direction-specific: (1) the sidecar records SANITIZED PRIM PATHS, not raw short names — probed live: `mayaUSDExport` writes `ref:nsCube` as `ref_nsCube`, so any NAMESPACED (i.e. referenced) instanced scene silently flattened before. Paths rather than leaf names because this direction's export keeps hierarchy: prim paths are unique, and Blender's `.001` collision suffix strips deterministically (a dot can never appear in a prim name, so a `.NNN` suffix is ALWAYS the importer's rename) — `/g1/wheel` vs `/g2/wheel` stays matchable even when the importer renames one. A path collision after sanitization (`ns:cube` beside `ns_cube`) fails the export loudly. (2) A failed replay rolls the import back out of the scene (`_rollback_import`: objects removed, displaced meshes purged) and raises; the bake raises before `save_as_mainfile`, so a flattened `.blend` is never cached; `bake_scene`/`import_scene` refuse upfront when the conversion's sidecar is missing. (3) The stray `mtl` Empty (BACKLOG 2026-08-02) is stripped PRIM-PATH-KEYED via `_strip_materials_scope`: only a root-level `Scope` prim whose whole subtree is material machinery qualifies (stage-inspected through Blender's bundled pxr), so a user object named `mtl` — or a real Xform prim named `mtl` — is untouched; cosmetic by contract (a strip failure logs and keeps everything), wired into the direct leg and the bake. Also fixed while in there: `bake_scene`'s docstring claimed `via="usd"` was the default (the signature says `"fbx"`), and the test pinning the default bake intermediate matched `.usd` against the template TEXT — vacuously true since the template's extension table contains ".usd"; it now checks the substituted `SRC_FILE` line. `test_scene_import.py` +20 checks (strict path-matching behavioral under bpy, scope strip incl. the user's-own-`mtl` case, atomic rollback); live e2e green on both routes.

- **2026-08-03 — Send to Maya no longer flattens the scene graph, and Empties arrive as the CORRECT Maya node types (live report: transform/locator hierarchy lost).** Three stacked causes, three fixes. (1) *The export set dropped the hierarchy*: every scope filtered Empties out ("Entire Scene"/"Visible Only" were mesh-only; "Selected" was the bare selection), and Blender's FBX exporter writes EXACTLY the given objects — an unlisted parent Empty is dropped and its children re-root, so the whole group structure silently flattened on the far side. Scopes now carry all object types (the export options decide what travels), and `BlenderExportMixin._hierarchy_closure` closes the set in `_produce`: ancestors always (the chain only — sending one mesh never widens to its siblings), descendants except under Visible Only (re-adding a hidden child would defeat the scope). Selecting a group Empty now means sending the group — parity with Maya's export-selection. The closed set rides `Payload.extras["export_set"]` so the manifest covers descendant meshes' materials too, and the strip-materials path re-parents its copies onto each other (they pointed at the unexported originals, so materials-off sends flattened even when the closure didn't). (2) *Every FBX null becomes a Maya locator, and the old repair demoted every parent to a group* — including locators that legitimately parent geometry. The manifest now carries an `empties` section (`name`, `empty_display_type`, and the `maya_node_type` custom property round-tripped scenes carry); the Maya-side rule: explicit tag wins outright, a non-`PLAIN_AXES` display type is a deliberate author marker and stays a locator EVEN WITH children, `PLAIN_AXES` falls through to the children heuristic (parent → group, leaf → locator). Applied in the `import` template (dependency-free copy incl. the `FBXASC` name encoding), `_save_scene.py`'s engine path, and `mtk.BlenderSceneImport._restore_empty_groups`. (3) *Round trips couldn't know what an Empty had been*: the pull direction (`MayaSceneImport`) now records `{leaf: "locator"|"group"}` in its conversion manifest (`scene_node_types` in `templates/_import_scene.py`) and stamps it as a `maya_node_type` custom property on the Empties it creates (`_tag_maya_node_types`; also in the `_bake_scene` reference path) — so Maya→Blender→Maya restores groups vs locators exactly. Live-verified end to end (Blender 5.1 → Maya 2025, headless save_as AND interactive GUI send): nested groups arrive as plain transforms with transforms intact, an ARROWS parent survives as a locator with children, leaves as locators, no sibling widening — new `test/send_live_e2e.py` pins all of it (plus the texture-rescue and OCIO legs; `-- --gui` adds the real `maya.exe` launch).

- **2026-08-03 — `MayaBridge.save_as("asset.ma")`: write a real Maya scene from Blender, no Maya window.** Mirror of mayatk's `BlenderBridge.save_as` (see its CHANGELOG and pythontk's for the shared seam). Same hand-off as the interactive send — identical FBX export, identical `.manifest.json` sidecar, `_produce` called once — delivered instead to a headless `mayapy` that imports the payload and saves. New `templates/_save_scene.py` carries the SAME two post-import repairs the interactive `import` template does, because the same FBX has the same two lossy spots: parent Empties lose their locator shape (delegated to `BlenderSceneImport._restore_empty_groups`, with a dependency-free fallback so a bare `mayapy` still saves a clean hierarchy) and materials are rebuilt from the sidecar through `_apply_texture_manifest`. `.ma` unless the path says `.mb` — ascii is diffable and survives a version bump. `objects=None` saves the whole scene (`_scene_objects`). The interpreter comes from `MayaBridge.headless_app_path`, derived from whatever `maya.exe` discovery (or the user) produced, so one path setting drives both routes and a send/save version mismatch is impossible; `mayapy_from_maya_exe` moved onto `MayaBridge` with the rest of the Maya discovery config (`MayaSceneImport` re-exports it), as did `_FAST_MAYA_ENV`, which the save now shares with the pull conversion. Saving OVER an existing scene is safe — the write is staged and promoted only on success (pythontk's CHANGELOG has the mechanism). Live-verified Blender 5.1 → Maya 2025: cube + parented Empty + material → a `.ma` in 5.8s, reopened in `mayapy` with the mesh (8 verts) shaded by `E2EMat` through its shading engine, and a pre-existing file at the target replaced rather than truncated. `test_maya_bridge.py` +16.

- **2026-08-03 — "Entire Scene" scope re-derived the scene instead of asking the bridge.** `BlenderBridgeSlotsBase.resolve_scope_objects` inlined `list(bpy.context.scene.objects)` — the same definition `BlenderExportMixin._scene_objects` now owns for `save_as`, so the panel's "Entire Scene" and the engine's whole-scene default were free to drift apart on the next edit to either. Routed through the hook (with the same `getattr` guard and geometry fallback as mayatk's resolver), which also restores the structural mirror after mayatk's twin moved.

- **2026-08-03 — Bridge parameter DEFAULTS moved to the Qt-free engine module — `save_as` has to work where Qt does not exist.** Headless Blender ships no Qt binding (verified: `import qtpy` fails under `--background --factory-startup`), and `params_defaults()` read its values out of `parameters.py`, which imports uitk → Qt. So the natural batch use of the new helper — convert a `.blend` to a `.ma` with no UI — would have died on a UI import. The values now live in `_maya_bridge.DEFAULTS` and the `AttributeSpec`s read THAT (one source of truth; a missing key is a loud import error, not drift), with `params_defaults` / `render_context` falling back to it and to pythontk's plain literal formatting when uitk is unavailable. Panel behavior unchanged; the whole `save_as` path is exercised inside headless Blender by `test_maya_bridge.py`.

- **2026-08-03 — Color ID tooltip silently truncated in the UI.** The `color_id.ui` tooltip read `ID_&lt;HEX&gt;`, which the .ui loader XML-decodes to a literal `ID_<HEX>` — Qt then rich-text-renders that as an unknown tag and eats it along with the rest of the sentence, so the artist never saw what the grouping actually does. Displaying a literal angle bracket needs DOUBLE escaping (`&amp;lt;`) so Qt receives `&lt;`. Caught by `check_tooltips.py`, which decodes exactly as Qt does; both DCC twins fixed.

- **2026-08-03 — Temp artifacts routed through `ptk.TempArtifacts`; two leaks fixed.** `HierarchySyncSlots._write_diff_report` wrote a diff report with `mkstemp` and never removed it (mirror of mayatk's twin), and `stage_reference_blend` staged a `.blend` that only the ERROR paths deleted — the success path, which is the common one, left it behind with nothing to reclaim it. Both now allocate detached so a later run sweeps stale leftovers by age. `BlenderConnection.run_code` and `SceneExporter`'s GLB staging dir keep their existing `finally` and gain the same safety net for the case it cannot run (a crash mid-operation). Gate: `m3trik/scripts/check_temp_artifacts.py`.

- **2026-08-03 — `MayaBridge._material_slots`: derive each texture's logical channel by TRACING the node graph, so Blender→Maya stops depending on filenames.**
  Maya's shaders declare their inputs, so the Maya→Blender legs could just read them. A Blender material has to be traced: every image node is walked FORWARD through its links to a Principled socket, crossing the converter nodes the wiring emits (Normal Map, Bump, Invert, Separate Color, AO multiply). Recorded only when the destination is UNAMBIGUOUS — zero channels means it feeds nothing resolvable, several means it is packed (an ORM reaching Metallic + Roughness + AO) and a packed map's identity lives in its filename, which Maya already reads correctly. Guessing one channel for it would be worse than silence. `Normal` is resolved by WHICH converter arrived: a Normal Map node means a normal map, a Bump node means bump/height — a distinction Maya's single `normalCamera` plug cannot express, so the shared vocabulary gained `bump`/`height` for it. Images inside node groups are not traced and simply yield no slot, degrading to today's filename-only behavior rather than to a wrong answer. `test_maya_bridge.py` +7.

- **2026-08-03 — Manifest replay rescues textures whose filenames carry no map-type token, and the Maya hand-off stops depending on the child's environment.**
  - **`_plan_with_slot_fallback`.** `_apply_texture_manifest` classified every manifest texture by filename via `ptk.MapFactory`, so a plain color map named after a product classified to nothing and the material stayed untextured. It now resolves the plan normally and consults the manifest's new `slots` **only** for files that landed in `unknown` — filename classification stays authoritative because only a filename reveals packing (`MSAO` in a metallic slot is still an MSAO), and a rescued type never displaces one the filename already resolved. Measured on the VDATS production scene: 3/9 → **9/9** materials rebuilt, verified by which SOCKET each image lands on.
  - **The PULL collector emits `slots` too** (`templates/_import_scene.py`). The applier is shared by both entry points, so the reference manager's `.ma`/`.mb` pull had the identical gap. Stingray declares its inputs, so the channel was already in hand there; the surface family is walked through history, where a file node has no reliable channel, and correctly emits nothing rather than guessing.
  - **`MayaBridge.render_context` threads `EXTRA_SYS_PATH`** (via `ptk.HandoffBridge.import_roots`), mirroring the fix mtk's Blender bridge needed. This leg happened to work only because Maya honors `PYTHONPATH` and mayatk was already on it — environment luck, not construction. A Maya without mayatk on its path hit the silent "mayatk unavailable; keeping the FBX-carried materials" branch, the same class of failure that made the Maya→Blender material fix inert.

- **2026-08-02 — Every Blender hand-off bridge now exposes the shared **Scope** combo (Selected / Entire Scene / Visible Only).** Mirror of mayatk's rollout (see its CHANGELOG). The resolver moves off the Unity slot onto `BlenderBridgeSlotsBase.resolve_scope_objects`, shared by the Maya / Unity / Marmoset / Substance / Rizom bridge slots, and the duplicated `AttributeSpec` is replaced by uitk's `Parameters.scope_spec()`. `"all"` deliberately reads `bpy.context.scene.objects` rather than `bpy.data.objects` — the latter also sweeps in unlinked/orphaned objects and objects belonging to other scenes. Each send site collapses to `self.scoped_objects(params)`; three now-dead `import blendertk as btk` statements went with the old selection calls. The Maya→Blender SEND direction also stops arriving unshaded — that fix is mayatk-side (texture sidecar replayed through this package's `MayaSceneImport._apply_texture_manifest`); see mayatk's CHANGELOG.

- **2026-08-02 — USD pull route, two live defects found on a production scene while USD was briefly the default: instancing collapsed material export, and every scene paid for animation it did not have.** Reported against `VDATS_RF_module.ma` (755 meshes) — the Open took minutes and the scene arrived under a `prototypes` collection with materials missing. Both causes measured, neither catchable by the old e2e (its trap scene is static and has no instanced shapes):
  - **`exportInstances` is now OFF.** With it on, mayaUsd's instance path collapses material export: on a probe scene whose only instances were two cubes, `def Material` went **3 → 0** and `material:binding` **4 → 0**, taking `mat_solo` — bound solely to a NON-instanced mesh — with it. It also delivered geometry as uneditable collection-instance Empties under a `prototypes` collection (the reported outliner), and dropped per-instance material assignments outright, since all instances share one prototype. **Flattening does NOT mean losing instances** — the relationship is recorded and rebuilt (below), so the route keeps materials, editability *and* the scene's memory profile.
  - **Instances survive as Blender linked duplicates, rebuilt from the sidecar.** Losing instancing is production-blocking, and USD itself cannot carry it: measured both ways, the importer turns instanceable prims into collection-instance Empties under a `prototypes` collection (uneditable), and `support_scene_instancing=False` gives real objects with **zero** data sharing. Neither is Blender's linked-duplicate model. (`mergeTransformAndShape=False` *does* restore materials under instancing — 0 → 3 — but writes one binding for the whole set and an Xform+Mesh pair per object, so it trades the material bug for a structural one.) So the export stays flat and the relationship travels as data: `collect_instance_groups` records every Maya instance set in the conversion sidecar, and the new `MayaSceneImport._apply_instance_manifest` points each set at ONE mesh datablock. Per-instance shaders survive — a member that disagreed with the master gets an OBJECT-linked material slot, Blender's own idiom — and the displaced meshes are freed so the sharing actually reclaims memory. **Measured on VDATS_RF: 755 objects → 628 datablocks / 10,208 verts / 755-of-755 shaded in 15.9s, against the FBX route's 627 / 10,103 / 755 in 18.2s** — parity on geometry, and faster. Wired into the `.blend` **bake** template too, since the Reference Manager's *Open* runs in its own headless Blender and would otherwise skip the rebuild (verified through `bake_scene`: 755 → 628). Recorded, never inferred: a geometry hash would fuse coincidentally-identical meshes and make editing one silently change another.
  - **The frame range is now derived from the animation, not the playback range.** USD has no animation *curves* — `frameRange` makes mayaUSDExport write a time sample per frame for EVERY prim, so the range is a direct multiplier on cost. The template passed the full playback range unconditionally, so a fully static production module was sampling 200 frames for a byte-identical result: **234s of a 268s conversion**. New `_animation_frame_range` samples the keyed span clamped to the playback range, returns `None` (static export) when nothing is animated, and still falls back to the full range for unkeyed drivers (constraint / expression / IK / motion path) whose motion can't be derived from key times. Set-driven keys (`animCurveU*`) are correctly excluded — their "times" are driver *values*, not frames. Measured: 5/5 logic cases, and 0.2s vs 4.0s on 40 objects keyed 1-20 inside a 1-1000 range.
  - **Net on the reported scene: 278s → 15.8s** (FBX route: 18.4s), 755/755 meshes shaded, zero `prototypes` collections. `wm.usd_import` was never the bottleneck (0.4s).
  - **The pull path now scrubs the OCIO hand-off** (pre-existing, found while verifying the above). `MayaSceneImport.convert` launches mayapy FROM Blender, so the child inherited Blender's `OCIO` — a `ocio_profile_version: 2.5` config Maya 2025's OCIO 2.3.2 cannot load, failing color-management init on *every* conversion (`This version of the OpenColorIO library (2.3.2) is not able to load that config version`). Both **send** paths were fixed for exactly this in an earlier pass; the **pull** path built its env by hand and was missed. It now reuses `MayaBridge._launch_env` (→ `ptk.AppLauncher.handoff_env`) rather than a second copy, so a studio config *outside* Blender's tree still passes through untouched. Verified: the e2e log goes from carrying the OCIO error to **zero** occurrences.
  - **Frame-range arithmetic uses floor/ceil, not `int()`** — `int()` truncates toward zero, so a key at 20.5 clipped to 20 (losing motion) and negative frames rounded the wrong way; and the keyed span is now read with ONE batched `cmds.keyframe` query instead of one round trip per curve (a rigged scene carries thousands). Both pinned: 8/8 cases incl. `20.5 → 21`, `-10.5 → -11`, and a 30-curve batch.
  - **Regression coverage:** the e2e gains an `e2e_inst_*` trap — an instanced shape whose two instances carry different materials — pinning no-prototypes-collection, no-instance-Empties, both instances as real meshes, every mesh still shaded, and per-instance materials staying distinct. Verified it *fails* (6 checks) with `exportInstances` restored and passes with the fix. Plus template pins in `test_scene_import.py` for both flags. Full e2e PASS on both routes; `test_scene_import` PASS.

- **2026-08-02 — Channels: auto-fit grew the window past the screen instead of scrolling, over-measured its own chrome, and was a verbatim copy of the mayatk implementation.** All three fixed at the root: the ~55 lines of Qt window measurement moved to uitk as `TableWidget.fit_window_to_contents()` (see uitk's CHANGELOG for the screen-fraction cap and the corrected `win - viewport` chrome measurement), and `_autofit_window` is now just the `Auto-fit Window` checkbox gate plus that one call. It was pure Qt with no `bpy` in it, so there was never a reason for this panel and its Maya twin to each carry a copy — they can no longer drift. The component-selection defect fixed alongside this in mayatk is Maya-only: Blender's selection is objects, never plug-style component strings.

- **2026-08-02 — Maya-scene pull: a complete USD route (`via="usd"`) alongside FBX, and the `.blend` bake accepts either intermediate. FBX remains the DEFAULT.** The default was flipped to USD mid-cycle and reverted the same day, before release, after a production scene exposed what the flip cost (see the top entry). The two routes now reach the same result — but they fail differently, and that decided it. FBX instancing is carried by the format itself on both sides (Blender's exporter shares one `Geometry` per mesh datablock; Maya's needs `FBXExportInstances -v true`, which the template sets), so nothing stands between a Maya instance set and Blender linked duplicates. USD reaches parity only by replaying a recorded grouping, and that replay is best-effort on all four legs: when it fails the scene *looks* correct and merely stops sharing data — a silent structural loss an artist discovers hours later, by which time they have edited one "duplicate" and watched its siblings ignore it. A failed FBX texture replay is loud (classic-model materials) and structurally harmless. Loud beats fast for a production default; USD's 15.9s vs 18.2s on VDATS_RF is real but not worth that trade. Making the instance replay loud is logged in `.claude/BACKLOG.md` as the prerequisite for revisiting. One nuance the two routes do NOT share: Blender→FBX splits linked duplicates that carry *different* modifier stacks (they are evaluated per object), where the USD sidecar groups on the mesh datablock alone. Changes: `MayaSceneImport.import_scene` / `bake_scene` / `convert` / `render_script` / `_cache_key` default `via="fbx"` (routes keep separate cache identities); `bake_scene` gains `via` and `_bake_scene.py` is source-generalized (`SRC_FILE`, extension dispatch: `.usd*` → kwarg-tolerant `wm.usd_import` plus the instance rebuild, with the texture/visibility replays bypassed; `.fbx` → the classic path, unchanged); `bake()`/`render_bake_script()` params renamed `fbx_path` → `src_path`. The Reference Manager grows a **"Foreign Scenes: Convert via USD / FBX"** header-menu combo (mirrored in mayatk, parity sweep clean) threaded through Open / Unlink-and-Import / link-bake via `_resolve_conversion`, and the Bake-vs-Raw prompt fires **only on the FBX route** — it exists to patch FBX's driven-animation hole, which USD samples natively (so with FBX as the default, that prompt keeps its pre-USD behavior). Combo item order is append-only — uitk persists a combo by INDEX, so the default moves via `setCurrentIndex`, never by reordering — and the objectName is `cmb_conversion_route`, renamed off `cmb_foreign_route` when the default changed so any already-stored index is orphaned rather than silently pinning a profile to the old route. Verified: `test_scene_import.py` (pinning the FBX default AND that `via="usd"` still renders the USD template, plus the USD-bake checks) / `test_usd.py` / `test_reference_manager.py` (82/82, incl. `_foreign_route` defaulting to FBX and a source pin tying the combo's `setCurrentIndex` to the actual FBX item, so reordering the append-only list fails the test instead of silently re-defaulting every profile); full suite **3298/3298 across 86 suites**; live e2e re-run — all five trap meshes green on BOTH routes (USD leg: standardSurface native, Stingray→standardSurface with packed metallic wired natively, project-relative textures resolved, live history evaluated, cache 12.0s→0.0s). Tentacle's "Import Maya Scene" slot inherits the route default (docstring updated). The e2e itself was repaired in passing: it still called the flat `btk.import_maya_scene`, dropped in the 07-19 encapsulation sweep — its FBX legs are now route-explicit `MayaSceneImport().import_scene(via="fbx")`.

- **2026-08-02 — Color ID's Outliner channel now sets the object's outliner TEXT color, like Maya's (`OutlinerTint`), and Set Per Color becomes an opt-in on both DCCs.** The previous day's mapping — link objects into a color-tagged `ID_<HEX>` collection — was correct about Blender's published API but diverged from mayatk: Maya's channel tints the object's *text*, and Blender exposes no equivalent (probed live on 5.1.2: no `Object.color_tag`/`outliner_color`/`use_outliner_color`, and `SpaceOutliner`'s whole RNA surface is filters and display toggles — no tree, rows, or expansion state). So the color is **painted**. New `display_utils/outliner_tint.py`: the color is stored as a `btk_outliner_color` custom property (ordinary data — saves with the .blend, readable without any of the below), and a `SpaceOutliner` `POST_PIXEL` draw handler walks the outliner's internal `TreeElement` tree through the space's struct pointer, masks each tinted row's label with the theme background, and repaints it in the object's color at Blender's own layout position (`text_x = te->xs + 2*UI_UNIT_X + 4*ufac`, `text_y = te->ys + 5*ufac`, from `outliner_draw.c`). Offsets were established empirically by planting known object names and matching the resolved IDs (`te->subtree` +0x20, `xs` +0x30, `ys` +0x34, `store_elem` +0x38 → `TreeStoreElem.id` +0x08 → `ID.name` +0x28, tree head at `SpaceOutliner` +0xC0). **`TreeElement` is runtime state with no API-stability guarantee, so the whole subsystem is built to fail closed**: the offsets are re-validated against the live process before the overlay is ever allowed to draw (the walk must resolve real `bpy.data` names, else it stands down), every dereference passes an OS memory-protection query (`_MemGuard`, region-cached — a bad offset yields `None`, never a segfault in the user's session), and any failure disables the overlay and leaves the outliner exactly as Blender drew it. A disabled overlay costs nothing and loses no data: the colors live on the objects. Non-Windows platforms keep the stored colors and skip the painting (`is_supported`). **Set Per Color (`chk016`, new on BOTH twins, off by default)** takes over the grouping the Outliner channel used to do — `ID_<HEX>` objectSet in Maya (`ColorId.add_to_color_set`/`get_color_set_color`/`remove_from_color_sets`, stamped `mtk_color_id`), color-tagged collection in Blender — as a grouping aid orthogonal to the four color channels. Both are stamp-keyed, never name-keyed, so a user's own `ID_*` set/collection is never adopted, retagged, or swept, and a recolor *moves* rather than piles up (emptied containers are garbage-collected; verified in mayapy that deleting an objectSet leaves its members alive). All four channels plus the new one now match mayatk by objectName, label, and capability — `compare_panel_surface --panel color_id` 0 untriaged / 0 prop deltas. Three details the overlay gets right because getting them wrong is visible or unsafe: the mask uses the row background Blender *actually* drew — plain back, or the selection/active highlight, keyed on **selected** (an active-but-unselected row has no highlight, so keying on "is active" alone paints a box where Blender drew none); the font comes from `ui_styles[0].widget.points`, not a hardcoded 11, so a user's UI font size still lines up; and a stand-down from inside the draw callback defers the `draw_handler_remove` to a timer rather than mutating Blender's handler list mid-iteration. `test_color_id.py` +13 checks (stored color round-trip, select-by, `tinted_objects`, reset, None-safety, and the enable/disable idempotency + "colors are readable regardless of the overlay" fail-closed contract) → 64/64; new GUI harness `test/outliner_tint_gui_check.py` covers what `--background` cannot (draw handlers never fire headless): live-process calibration reaching `status == "ok"`, the walk resolving every planted object at distinct row positions, selected/active/unselected rows all masked correctly, a deliberately corrupted offset standing the overlay down with a recorded reason instead of crashing (colors intact), and a screenshot → 16/16.
- **2026-08-02 — Maya bridge send: hierarchy arrived as locators, real materials arrived gray, and the launched Maya inherited Blender's OCIO. All three fixed (live-verified end to end).**
  - **Parent Empties → plain Maya groups.** Blender Empties travel as FBX nulls and Maya's importer gives EVERY null a locator shape, so a sent group tree arrived as locators all the way down. The `import` template now strips the locator shape from every imported transform whose Empty has children (a Blender Empty *is* a Maya group); a CHILDLESS Empty stays a locator — it marks a point, and a shapeless leaf transform would be invisible. Scoped to the import's new nodes (pre-existing user locators untouchable), skips multi-shape transforms. The import itself went deterministic while at it: `FBXResetImport` + pinned `add` mode + `returnNewNodes` (mirror of the pull engine's `_import_fbx` — sticky global import options and the factory `merge` mode could retarget animation onto same-named scene nodes).
  - **Native material rebuild via the pull direction's sidecar contract.** Blender's FBX exporter only carries images wired (almost) directly into Principled sockets — packed ORM/MSAO through SeparateColor, AO multiplies, node-group plumbing export as NOTHING, so production materials (exactly what the Maya→Blender direction *builds*) arrived gray. `MayaBridge._produce` now writes the same `.manifest.json` the pull has always used (in-process collector: whole-node-tree walk incl. groups, UDIM flatten-to-first-tile, file-less entries for packed-only images so Maya warns BY NAME, `scene_materials` for the rename-on-clash guard; kept in step by hand with the dependency-free copy in mayatk's pull template), and the import template replays it through the ONE existing applier, `mayatk.BlenderSceneImport._apply_texture_manifest` (GameShader → standardSurface, the established template→paired-engine contract from both `_bake_scene.py` templates) — guarded, so a Maya without mayatk keeps the FBX materials with a console line saying so. Skipped when `INCLUDE_MATERIALS` is off.
  - **Launch env.** The launched Maya inherited Blender's whole environment; an `OCIO` pointing at Blender's bundled v2.5 config fails Maya 2025's color-management init on every send ("This version of the OpenColorIO library (2.3.2) is not able to load that config version"). `_SPEC.launch_env` → `MayaBridge._launch_env` strips OCIO via the new `ptk.AppLauncher.handoff_env` exactly when it points inside Blender's own install (root from `bpy.app.binary_path`; import-safe outside Blender); a studio config elsewhere inherits untouched.
  - Verified live: probe scene (`grp > sub > cube` + leaf Empty + a Mix-routed texture the FBX provably drops) exported through the real producer, imported in mayapy through the real rendered template — arrives as `grp/sub` plain groups, `leaf_marker` locator, and a rebuilt `standardSurface` with the texture wired. `test_maya_bridge.py` +10 → 25 (template wiring pins; manifest schema incl. shared-datablock merge, packed → file-less, clash guard; launch-env all three branches). scene_import (74) / unity_bridge (15) re-run green.

- **2026-08-02 — Bridge hand-offs flattened the scene graph: the shared FBX export set was mesh-only, so every Empty was dropped and its children re-rooted.** `fbx_utils._EXPORT_DEFAULTS` pinned `object_types={"MESH"}`, and Blender's FBX exporter does not just omit an excluded type — it **re-parents that object's children to the nearest included ancestor**, i.e. to the root. A `grp > sub > mesh` chain therefore arrived in Maya (and Unity) as parentless meshes: total, silent loss of the scene graph on every send. Every other consumer had already discovered this and overridden it locally (Substance and Marmoset pin `{"MESH","EMPTY"}`, the Scene Exporter `["EMPTY","ARMATURE","MESH"]`, tentacle's scene slot `{"MESH","EMPTY","OTHER"}`) — the bridges were the ones left on the broken default. `EMPTY` is now in the shared default (a Blender Empty *is* a Maya group; hierarchy is not an opt-in), and `BlenderExportMixin._fbx_options` — the producer behind the Maya and Unity bridges — widens it to `{"MESH","EMPTY","ARMATURE","OTHER"}`, covering the other two silent losses: skin deformation (the mirror of mayatk's `FBXExportSkins -v true`) and curve/text/metaball geometry. Cameras and lights stay out; the hand-off ships assets. Verified live on 5.1.2 in both directions — the Blender→Maya round trip now returns `grp > sub > mesh` intact, and the Maya→Blender pull (whose MEL flag battery was always correct) is unchanged. `test_fbx_utils.py` +3 (parenting round-trip through the defaults; the hand-off set; and a `BlenderExportMixin._export_fbx` round trip proving a `grp > armature > skinned mesh` chain survives the real producer). Substance and Marmoset are untouched — their `_produce` calls `export_selection_fbx` directly with their own options, so only the two DCC-to-DCC bridges read `_fbx_options`. bridges / maya_bridge / unity_bridge / scene_import / scene_exporter / emissive_groups / rizom suites re-run green.

- **2026-08-02 — Workspace Editor review: a rule added in Nice Names view wrote the LABEL as the rule name, table edits died after a UI reload, and the root field acted on every keystroke.** Companion to the mayatk half (see its CHANGELOG for the shared model work).
  - **Nice-name rule keys.** A row added via *Add New File Rule* has no stored key, so `_key_at` fell back to its cell text — and in Nice Names view the user is looking at labels, so they type one. "Render Data" went into `workspace.mel` verbatim as `workspace -fr "Render Data" …`, a rule name Maya does not recognize. New `_key_for_label` reverse-maps `ptk.RULE_NICE_NAMES` case-insensitively; an unknown label still passes through as a custom rule.
  - **Signals re-wire on every `_init`.** `tbl000`'s `itemChanged` was wired inside the one-time `if not is_initialized` block with no `refresh_on_show`, the exact latent gap the channels panel was fixed for: the QWidget outlives the Slots instance, so after a UI reload every cell edit was bound to a dead `self` and silently no-op'd. Both `tbl000` and `txt000` now set `refresh_on_show` and route their connects through an idempotent `_rewire` (disconnect-then-connect), with only widget-state setup left in the one-time block.
  - **Root field is debounced.** `textChanged` fired per keystroke, and the handler reloads the rule table *and pins the session's current workspace* — so typing a path pinned every partial path that happened to exist along the way. A plain `defer_with_timer` would not have fixed it (`QTimer.singleShot` stacks rather than debounces), so the deferred call carries a generation stamp and all but the last no-op. Enter still commits immediately.
  - **Create Project** is now an explicit header-menu verb. Building a project only ever happened as a side effect of editing a rule — `open_folder` even said so ("edit a rule to create it"), which is a strange thing to tell someone who just asked for File ▸ Project Window ▸ New.
  - **Templates moved to `ptk.WorkspaceTemplates`** — the shared unnamespaced store — so a template saved here also builds Maya projects (`mtk.create_workspace`). `btk.list/save/delete_workspace_template` and `workspace_template_rules` are now thin mirrors of it; `save_workspace_template(name)` with no rules captures the current workspace's own rules, matching the mayatk twin.
  - **`promote_workspace` / `current_workspace` shed their hand-rolled halves.** The layout-describing heuristics and the path→workspace resolver tail were duplicated verbatim in mayatk; both now delegate to the new `ptk.Workspace.promote` / `.for_path` (see pythontk's CHANGELOG), so the two DCCs can no longer describe the same folder differently.
  - `test_workspace.py` +10 → 46; reference_manager (76) and blender_ui_handler (226) re-run green. Parity sweep exit 0.

- **2026-08-02 — Freeze/unfreeze audit (mayatk parity): the axis fixer stamped no history, two origin moves left a STALE location bake, and the stored history is now readable + prefixable.** Blender half of the same sweep; see mayatk's CHANGELOG for the shared design.
  - **`fix_non_orthogonal_axes` recorded nothing.** `parent_clear(CLEAR_KEEP_TRANSFORM)` / `visual_transform_apply()` rewrite the local transform without going through `freeze_transforms`, so an axis fix was irreversible — while the mayatk twin has always stamped. It stamps all three channels now (these ops write the WHOLE local transform, unlike Maya's rotate/scale-only freeze).
  - **Stale location bakes.** Blender's origin IS the translate reference, so ANY origin move invalidates a stored freeze-location bake — leaving one makes a later Un-Freeze **double-apply the translation**. `center_pivot` already dropped it, but `EditUtils.separate_objects(center_pivots=True)` and tentacle's Bake Pivot called `bpy.ops.object.origin_set` directly and skipped the drop. The invalidation is now `_XformUtilsInternal._invalidate_location_bake` and both call sites route through `XformUtils.center_pivot`, which gains a `"cursor"` mode (Maya's Bake Pivot) so the slot has a sanctioned path. `center_pivot` is documented as the only origin-move entry point.
  - **Bake keys take a `prefix`** (mirroring mayatk): `_bake_keys(prefix)` backs `_store_bakes` / `restore_transforms` / `has_stored_transforms`, so a tool that re-bases a local frame for its own bookkeeping can never compose onto the user's freeze history. New public **`XformUtils.store_transforms`** (the stamp side, which blendertk lacked entirely) and **`get_stored_transforms`** (the read side).
  - **New `"original"` pivot mode** on `get_operation_axis_matrix`: rebuilds the pre-freeze frame as `matrix_world @ stored` (column-vector convention), falling back to `"object"` when unstamped. A frozen object's local axes are the world's, so `"object"` alone could not express the frame the asset was authored in.
  - **New `restore_original_axes`** — Maya aims its manipulator pivot; the Blender parity is a custom transform orientation (the same slot World-Aligned Pivot flips to `GLOBAL`), written from the pre-freeze frame and made active. Non-destructive; no-ops headless, where `create_orientation` has no screen context. Surfaced as "Restore Authored Axes" in the Un-Freeze option box.
  - **Auto-instancer** `center_transform_on_geometry` / `canonicalize_transform` stamp under `CANONICAL_BAKE_PREFIX`, so the PCA re-basing no longer destroys the authored frame unrecoverably.
  - **`freeze_transforms` now RETURNS the objects it actually baked** (mirroring `restore_transforms`), empty when everything was skipped. Without it a caller cannot tell a fully-skipped multi-user batch from a successful one — the engine skips linked data with a console message where the raw operator used to raise, so tentacle's World-Aligned Pivot button would have looked like it did nothing. `_bake_keys` moved onto `_XformUtilsInternal` (no root-level functions).
  - `test_xform_utils.py` +13, `test_diagnostics.py` +2. Full suite 3219/3219; panel parity sweep clean.

- **2026-08-02 — Unity Bridge: Manage Unity Scripts lists the C# scripts as a checkable set, and the Action applies to whatever is checked.** Mirrors mayatk 1:1 (same parameter registry entry, same slot methods; `compare_panel_surface --panel unity_bridge` clean). New **`SCRIPTS`** parameter (uitk's `check_list` kind) fills itself at panel init from `unitytk.TemplateDeployer.components()` — one checked row per import channel with its own tooltip — so Install / Update deploys only the checked controllers (the shared core files ride along), Uninstall removes only those (dropping the package folder with the last one), and Status reports every component's state regardless of the checkboxes. `_manage_unity_scripts` keeps only the panel-side preconditions; the work and its wording come from `TemplateDeployer.run_action`, shared with the mayatk and extapps Unity panels. Verified building the panel outside Blender (Qt-only): rows, default checks and the manage-mode gate all match mayatk's.

- **2026-08-02 — Game Shader: 1:1 parity with mayatk — the map pipeline is live, and the two dead controls are gone rather than faked.** The Blender panel shipped four disabled widgets; three of those reasons were wrong or stale. **Output Template (`cmb002`) and Ext (`cmb003`) are now ENABLED and wired.** The ledger claimed Blender "wires existing textures and never writes output maps", but the pipeline behind both combos is `ptk.MapFactory.prepare_maps` / `ptk.MapRegistry`, which live in DCC-agnostic **pythontk**, and the sibling `MatUpdater` in this very package already ran it. A new **`GameShader` engine class** (`mat_utils/game_shader.py`, registered as `btk.GameShader` ↔ `mtk.GameShader`) now mirrors `create_network` name-for-name: resolve a workflow profile (Unity URP/HDRP, Unreal, glTF, Godot …), run `prepare_maps` (format conversion, resize/optimize, **packed-map generation**), resolve one source per input, and delegate the graph build to `MatUtils.create_pbr_material`. Pillow is provisioned on demand by `btk.ensure_image_deps`; without it the profile/format steps are skipped with a warning and the textures wire as-is — degraded, never a hard failure. Verified live on 5.1.2: an "Unreal Engine" profile over loose BaseColor/Normal/Roughness/Metallic/AO now **writes a packed `_ORM.png` and wires it**, which the panel could not do before.
  **Correctness fix.** `create_pbr_material` decided packed-vs-loose with a local chain (`ORM` pops `MSAO` pops `Metallic_Smoothness`) that was a blendertk-only invention. It now calls the SHARED `ptk.MapFactory.filter_redundant_maps` through a new `MatUtils.resolve_pbr_plan` — the same SSoT mayatk's `_resolve_map_conflicts` uses — so the two DCCs cannot drift, and the shared filter is *lossless*: a channel no surviving loose map covers is **extracted to a real file** instead of discarded (an MSAO beside loose Metallic/Roughness no longer loses its AO). Two behavior changes fall out, both toward Maya: `ORM` and `MSAO` are different channel layouts and neither supersedes the other (the old chain wrongly dropped MSAO), and reporting now distinguishes superseded / unrecognized / classified-but-unwirable maps. Reporting reached parity too: config banner, per-map table with conversion notes, batch summary, `progress_callback` → footer, and a clickable material link via a new `graph` action on `UiUtils.dispatch_log_link` (opens the Shader Editor — the analogue of Maya's Hypershade select).
  **Four defects the shared-filter switch exposed, all fixed with tests.** (1) The registry lets a packed **`MRAO`** supersede loose Metallic/Roughness/AO — but `create_pbr_material` had no MRAO consumer, so such a set would have dropped its loose maps and wired *nothing* in their place; MRAO is now wired (R→Metallic, G→Roughness, B→AO) like ORM. (2) `ORM` and `MSAO` can now coexist (neither supersedes the other) and each multiplies AO into Base Color, and `_multiply_into_base` *chains* onto the existing link — two surviving packed maps stacked two MULTIPLY nodes and darkened the albedo twice; AO is now applied once. (3) The per-map table inferred “connected” from the plan, so a map the wiring deliberately skips because another already drives its input (Height beside a Normal, Glossiness beside a real Roughness) was reported ✓ — precisely the silent drop the table exists to prevent. `create_pbr_material` now records the map types it actually wired into `plan["wired"]` (keyed off the image-load site, so there is no per-site bookkeeping to drift) and those rows report `– <Owner> already drives that input`. (4) An explicitly tagged **`Normal_OpenGL`** map was green-flipped whenever the panel's combo said DirectX, inverting an already-correct map; a filename tag now outranks the combo, which only decides the ambiguous `Normal` case (mayatk was already correct here — it prefers a map in the desired format rather than flipping unconditionally — so this was a Blender-only bug and the reference DCC is untouched).
  **`chk000` (AiBridge) and `cmb004` (Shader Type) were REMOVED from the `.ui`,** not left disabled. AiBridge attaches a parallel `aiStandardSurface` preview network; Blender ships no Arnold integration. Shader Type picks a Maya shader *node* — and probing Blender 5.1.2 settled the OpenPBR question: there is **no OpenPBR node** (0 of 101 shader node types), but Principled **is** the OpenPBR model — its inputs map onto `ND_open_pbr_surface_surfaceshader`, MaterialX 1.39.4 ships bundled, and Blender's own USD export emits `ND_open_pbr_surface_surfaceshader` for a Principled material, with every conversion node this tool builds translating cleanly (`MixRGB`→`ND_mix_color3`, `Invert`→`ND_subtract_color3`, `SeparateColor`→`ND_extract_color3`, `NormalMap`→`ND_normalmap_float`). So OpenPBR is reachable, and what the Maya combo really selects is the downstream *target* — which on this panel is `cmb002`. A Blender shader-type combo would be a second control for the same decision, with two items naming Maya shaders that are never created; a dead control that says "Stingray PBS" misleads a Maya user more than its absence does. Both are ledgered `na` in `tentacle/docs/parity_map.py`; the panel now has **zero disabled widgets** and the sweep reports 0 untriaged / 0 prop deltas / 0 item deltas.

- **2026-08-02 — Telescope Rig: parity pass with mayatk — optional handles, two-segment struts, auto collapse, a real teardown, and a build that no longer teleports the segments.** Ported alongside the mayatk change (see its CHANGELOG for the shared design). Blender-specific work: the build had **no teardown at all** (Maya has had one since the 2026-07-18 overhaul), so it now records a `TelescopeRigBundle` — constraint names, driver paths, created Empties, and the prior locks/location/scale it overwrote — returns it instead of the bare segment list, stamps it as JSON on the base handle (`telescope_rig_data`), and exposes `teardown`/`scene_bundles`/`find_bundles` + a `btn_remove` slot. Three behavior fixes: (1) every segment was positioned by `Copy Location` with no offset, so the build **snapped each segment's origin onto the lerp line** — harmless when the handles happened to sit on the segment pivots, actively destructive now that omitted handles are auto-placed at the strut's *outer ends*. End segments now ride their handle with `Child Of` (scale channels off — Maya's `parentConstraint` drives translate/rotate only) and interiors solve the offset that keeps the build pose exactly, `o = (pose - frac*end)/(1 - frac) - base`, written to the pre-constraint location because that is what `use_offset` adds; teardown restores it.
