Also fixes the **count**: the add/clear operators return `{'CANCELLED'}` for a mesh already in the target state — they do **not** raise — so the old `except RuntimeError` guard never fired and the loop counted every mesh it visited as cleared (clearing two never-locked meshes reported "2 cleared"). Both directions now skip meshes already in the target state, so the count means what it says. `has_custom_split_normals` mirrors the all-or-nothing state read of Maya's `m_lock_vertex_normals` (a mixed selection reads unlocked, so a toggle locks it whole). `test_edit_utils.py` gains the round-trip, no-op-count, mixed-selection, Edit-Mode, and non-mesh cases — the previous coverage asserted only `n >= 0`, which is why the miscount slipped through; red-first against the old body it reported 2.

- **2026-07-17 — Slot-audit fix sweep: every ops-backed helper now works from tentacle's windowless Qt-pump state and from Edit Mode.** The `_object_mode` guard runs its whole body under `window_context_override()` — the ops it wraps (`origin_set`, `modifier_apply`, `select_all`, `mesh.separate`, custom-normals clear …) read screen-context members that are dead when a slot fires from the Qt event-pump timer, and the guard's own `mode_set` poll-failed from Edit Mode in that state, so decimate/boolean/combine/pivot/shading helpers either raised or silently acted on nothing in live use. `separate_objects`, `center_pivot`, and `average_normals` were missing the guard entirely (their siblings had it) and gained it; `select_by_material` deselects via `view_layer.objects` and skips view-layer-excluded users instead of crashing mid-loop on multi-scene files. New public API: `apply_subdivision(objects)` — bakes the live Subsurf (the destructive counterpart of `set_subdivision`, Maya's smooth-preview→polygons; `show_viewport`-gated so a disabled modifier can't abort the batch) — and `Selection.loop_multi_select(ring=)`, resolving Blender 5.0's split `select_edge_loop_multi`/`select_edge_ring_multi` names with the 4.x `loop_multi_select(ring=)` fallback. `set_shading`/`flip_normals` grew `selected_only` (Maya component-scope parity) with face-precedence scoping — `bmesh.from_mesh` flushes a face selection down to its boundary edges, so a naive edge read spills shading onto neighboring faces (caught headless, red-first). New permanent suite `test/test_windowless_ops.py` reproduces the pump state via `temp_override(window=None)` (same technique as `test_core_utils.py`), including the precondition that a bare `origin_set` is a no-op there; 78/78 suites pass.

- **2026-07-17 — uitk restructure sync (mirror of mayatk's): deep imports of `uitk.widgets.mixins.{settings_manager,state_manager,icon_manager,preset_manager}` → `uitk.managers.*`; case-broken `.ui` custom-widget headers (`uitk.widgets.combobox` / `table_widget`) corrected to the real `comboBox` / `tableWidget` module casing.** Path sync only, no behavior change — uitk moved its standalone services out of `widgets/mixins/` into `uitk/managers/` (+ `uitk/themes/`); details in uitk's CHANGELOG.

- **2026-07-17 — Mirror of mayatk's structure cleanup: `ui_utils/blender_bridge_slots.py` → `blender_bridge_slots_base.py`.** The file holds `BlenderBridgeSlotsBase` (file↔class convention), and the `_base` suffix keeps the shared Output-Dir-fallback base visibly distinct from the `<tool>_bridge_slots.py` *panel* files — the old scheme made mayatk's `ui_utils/maya_bridge_slots.py` (base) and blendertk's `env_utils/maya_bridge/maya_bridge_slots.py` (panel) character-identical filenames with different roles. Importers updated (unity/marmoset/substance). `docs/STRUCTURE.md` gains the previously missing `render_utils` ⛔ row (mayatk-only engine; create-on-demand per the ⛔ rule), the bridge-slots-base filename pair in the `ui_utils` row, and a note ledgering the `shader_templates` layout divergence (mayatk: subpackage anchoring shipped `templates/*.yaml`; blendertk: flat module over the shared `pythontk.PresetStore`).

- **2026-07-16 — Native-menu wrappers are now named `<name>_menu`, not the bare mapping name — 'render' and 'window' were shadowing `QWidget.render()` / `QWidget.window()` on the window that hosts them.** The mirror of the mayatk fix (surfaced there by a live `objectName 'render' shadows MainWindow.render()` warning; blendertk had the same latent defect, with 'window' as the extra offender). `MainWindow.register_widget` binds every registered child's objectName as an attribute on the window, and `get_menu` named the `EmbeddedMenuWidget` after its `MENU_MAPPING` key — so for the colliding keys uitk's shadow guard warned and skipped the binding. Nothing read the objectName (lookups go through `self.menus[name]`), so the rename is contained to the registration handle. New `test/test_blender_native_menus.py` (2 checks) drives the real `get_menu` with only the bpy-side harvest mocked, over **every** mapping entry rather than the two that collide today; red-first it reported `{'render': 'render', 'window': 'window'}`. Qt-not-bpy, so it's a `.venv` target that skips with a PASS sentinel under the Blender harness, like `test_blender_ui_handler.py`.

- **2026-07-16 — Pre-push review sweep on this change set.** `toggle_editor` no longer claims "now shown" when nothing was: since `open_editor` learned to return `None` on poll refusal (windowless Qt-timer context, `--background`, fullscreen screen), the fallback branch now returns `open_editor(...) is not None` instead of a blanket `True`, so a caller keying UI state off the toggle can't desync. `target_weld`'s guarded module-top `import bpy` moved into call bodies — the module was already headless-import-safe, but every other module (and the CLAUDE.md rule) defers, and the docstring said so. `ScriptConsole.show` now logs the bare-Info-Log degrade on non-Windows platforms too (the module's "observable degrade" contract only covered the Windows no-QApplication case). Two stale `MacroManagerSlots` docstring references in `macros.py` updated to `Macros.show_editor` (the panel this change set retires). New `test/test_menu_harvest.py` (16 checks, venv + Blender harness) pins the harvest recorder's pure logic where it lives — `_OpProps` nested macro-props recording (the dead Vertices/Edges menus fix), `_plain_props` empty-group dropping, `_LayoutRecorder` shared-items/greying/swallow semantics, and `_MenuShim` binding rules (helpers bound, staticmethods not).

- **2026-07-16 — Script Output: resizing the docked console no longer grabs a text selection (live report: "the cursor changes to the resize arrow but it grabs a text selection instead").** Root cause is a hit-zone overlap baked into the embed geometry: `QtDock` glued the child window flush over the docked area's WINDOW region, but Blender's area-edge grab band extends ± `BORDERPADDING` (`screen_geometry.cc`: `2·UI_SCALE_FAC + U.pixelsize`, ~3px each side at 1x) *into* both neighboring areas — so the band's lower half sat on top of the embedded `QTextEdit`. The OS cursor read "resize" over the remaining sliver of Blender-owned border, but a press that landed one pixel lower went to the Qt child and started a selection drag; the workable target was a ~2px line. `QtDock._position` now insets the child's TOP by that native tolerance (`_native_edge_pad`, resolved from live preferences at `dock()` so it tracks UI scale) whenever the WINDOW region is flush with the area's top edge (`_expose_resize_edge`; a shown header already provides its own buffer → no inset) — every pixel where the resize cursor shows stays Blender-owned, so hover and click agree and the exposed strip reads as a slightly thicker splitter. Side edges deliberately stay flush (grabbable along their full height in the taller neighbors); the persisted strip height is untouched (it reads `area.height`, not the child rect). New `test/test_qt_dock.py` (10 checks, venv + Blender harness): the inset math (flush / header / zero-pad / tiny-strip / dead-wrapper), the pad resolving from real 5.1 preferences, and the load-bearing live assumption that a docked strip's WINDOW region tops out AT the area top (headless note: `screen.area_close` blows the stack under `--background` on 5.1, so the suite leaves teardown to the fresh-instance harness). GUI harnesses updated to the new contract (child rect = region minus the strip): `console_dock_check.py` / `editors_toggle_check.py`.

- **2026-07-16 — Maya-scene import: production-scene fixes (live report: everything pink + "_fbxsafe1..13" material explosion on a 403-object scene).** Two template bugs, both reproduced red-first in the e2e before fixing: **(1) One phong per source MATERIAL, not per shading group** — production scenes routinely feed one material into many SGs (per-object SG splits, merged imports); the per-SG translation created N duplicate phongs, N duplicate manifest entries, and an N-material pile-up in Blender. `fbx_safe_materials` now memoizes: every SG of an already-translated material reuses its phong, and the manifest entry merges member objects (pinned by a new `e2e_shared` trap — one standardSurface through two SGs must arrive as ONE material). **(2) The scene's Maya project is now opened before converting** (`_resolve_workspace`: nearest workspace.mel above the scene → `cmds.workspace(openWorkspace)`) — headless Maya otherwise can't resolve project-relative texture paths, so the FBX carried unresolvable strings (Blender's image search only looks near the FBX, which sits in temp → pink everything) and the manifest collected no files. The earlier "no workspace handling needed" conclusion was the e2e layout's luck, not a general truth; the e2e docstring now records the correction. Also: the silent `continue` on file-less manifest entries is gone — the engine now warns per material whether the paths never resolved during conversion (project missing / relink needed) or resolved but are missing on disk, so pink is never unexplained (critique catch: the manifest now *includes* file-less entries — the old write-side filter dropped exactly the materials that needed the warning, leaving that diagnostic path dead); the template prints its resolved workspace and a per-material texture-count line into the captured conversion output. **(3) Cache-key hardening (critique catch):** the conversion cache key now includes the template's own identity (mtime + size) — without it, a template fix would replay stale cached payloads and look unfixed on retry. `test_scene_import.py` pins the workspace + memoization template contract (+2 checks).

- **2026-07-16 — Maya-scene import: performance pass ("the process is a bit slow") — conversion cache, no more texture embedding, fast-startup mayapy env.** Three structural sinks, each addressed where the time actually goes: **(1) Conversion cache** (`use_cache=True` default) — the payload is keyed by scene path + mtime + size + Maya-side options and stored under a detached-policy `TempArtifacts` prefix (`maya_to_btk_cache_*`, deterministic fixed-name tags; the existing 7-day stale sweep IS the cache GC — no new machinery). A hit skips the mayapy launch (and its license checkout) entirely: **measured live 10.9s → 0.0s** on the identical-scene re-import, the iterate workflow's common case. **Poison-proof by construction** (critique-pass fix): conversion always targets the scoped scratch path and only a *completed* conversion is `os.replace`-promoted (atomic, same volume) into the cache slot — a timeout-killed partial FBX write stays in scratch (kept + logged) and can never become a sticky corrupt "hit"; concurrent imports of the same scene can't interleave into one file, and a good cached FBX survives a Blender-side import failure for retry. Texture edits still flow through on a hit because of (2). **(2) `embed_textures` now defaults OFF** — embedding was pure waste on this same-machine flow *since the manifest rebuild landed*: every texture was copied into the FBX, unpacked by Blender, then discarded when the rebuild re-pointed at the originals on disk (and orphan-purged). Un-embedded, the FBX carries file references Blender resolves from disk — cost no longer scales with texture size, and the cache stays correct under texture edits (Blender always loads current files). Kept as an opt-in for genuine cross-machine hand-offs. Pinned live: all four e2e texture traps green with embedding off. **(3) Fast-startup child env** — the conversion mayapy runs with `MAYA_SKIP_USERSETUP_PY=1` (a pipeline userSetup can bootstrap a whole toolkit a one-shot converter never touches) + the `MAYA_DISABLE_CIP/CER/CLIC_IPM` analytics trio (network round-trips); scene *requirements* (Arnold/USD module plugins) are untouched — `cmds.file(open)` still resolves them. The `_run_script` seam gained `env=` passthrough. `test_scene_import.py` pins cache hit/miss/`use_cache=False`, the fast env, and the scoped-vs-cached cleanup split (+3 checks); the e2e pins the cache-hit speedup ratio and excludes by-design cache files from its leftovers sweep.

- **2026-07-16 — Maya-scene import: packed PBR maps (Metallic_Smoothness / MSAO / ORM) now arrive in Blender — a texture-set manifest sidecar rebuilt through the game-shader engine.** FBX's classic material model has **no slot at all** for metallic/roughness/ao data, packed or separate — the phong translation could never carry them. Rather than growing a bespoke wiring layer, the fix composes two things the ecosystem already ships: the conversion template (throwaway scene, dependency-free) now writes ``<out>.fbx.manifest.json`` beside the payload — per translated material: the ORIGINAL texture files (Stingray read from its declared ``TEX_*`` slots so the env-cube/BRDF plumbing stays out; the standardSurface family walks history) plus the assigned transform names — and after the FBX import, ``MayaSceneImport._apply_texture_manifest`` feeds each entry's file list to **``btk.create_pbr_material``** (the engine under the game_shader panel), which classifies by filename via the shared ``ptk.MapFactory`` SSoT — the *same classifier GameShader used to build these Stingray networks in the first place* — and wires the full Principled graph: ``Metallic_Smoothness`` (RGB→Metallic, A→invert→Roughness), the HDRP ``MSAO`` mask, ``ORM``, gloss→roughness, AO-multiply. Conservative by construction: only *translated* (FBX-hostile) materials get manifest entries; an entry whose files classify to nothing keeps its FBX-carried phong (logged) — pinned live by the e2e's oddly-named standardSurface set; per-entry failures degrade and the whole apply is call-site guarded (a malformed sidecar can never abort an import whose FBX already landed); the sidecar rides the same scoped temp lifecycle as the FBX. **Application is a SLOT-level swap** keyed by the translated phong's name (`fbx_material`, unique per shading group) — multi-material/per-face meshes keep their layout (`assign_mat` would clobber every slot; pinned live by a half-phong half-Stingray cylinder) and duplicate object leaf names can't cross-assign; object-level assign remains only as the importer-renamed-the-material fallback. Replaced phong materials and their now-exclusive embedded images are purged once unused (best-effort `_purge_orphans`) so the rebuild leaves no datablock litter. Verified end-to-end: the e2e's StingrayPBS object now carries a conventionally named set + a packed ``Metallic_Smoothness`` map with `use_metallic_map` on, and the imported Blender material has **Principled Metallic linked** through the packed chain (red before the manifest path, green after). Rebuilt materials reference the original on-disk texture paths (same behavior as the game_shader panel itself). ``test_scene_import.py`` pins the manifest write, the rebuild-and-assign orchestration, missing-file skip, and sidecar cleanup (+4 checks).

- **2026-07-16 — Maya-scene import: StingrayPBS (ShaderFX game shader) materials now carry their textures — the second live texture report ("material link b'Maya|TEX_color_map' ignored").** The first texture fix covered the standardSurface family; the user's actual scene used **StingrayPBS** (the GameShader tool's output), which Maya's FBX exporter writes as a ``Maya|TEX_*`` custom-property set that Blender's importer ignores outright — hence the warnings and gray materials. Repro'd red-first by a fourth e2e trap object (StingrayPBS + color map, batch-created via ``shaderfx initShaderAttributes`` since ``shadingNode`` alone skips the UI's default-graph init). ``fbx_safe_materials`` now dispatches by node type: the standardSurface branch (`_translate_surface`) and a new ``_translate_stingray`` branch — ``TEX_color_map``→color, ``TEX_normal_map``→tangent-space ``bump2d``→normalCamera, ``TEX_emissive_map``→incandescence, each honoring its ``use_*_map`` toggle (a wired-but-disabled map stays off), and the emissive map additionally gated on ``emissive_intensity`` > 0 (the symmetric bug to the standardSurface emission-weight gate: intensity 0 emits nothing in Maya and must not glow in Blender); ``base_color``/``roughness`` scalars approximated (shared ``_roughness_to_cosine_power``). **Honest limit, logged per material:** metallic / roughness / ao MAPS have no slot in FBX's classic material model and are dropped with a "not carried" line in the conversion output — carrying them would need a glTF/sidecar route, not FBX. Verified live: the four-trap e2e is fully green (Stingray trap red before, green after); ``test_scene_import.py`` pins the new branch (+1 check).

- **2026-07-16 — Maya-scene import: textures now ride the FBX — modern surface shaders are translated to phong before export, plus the full-fidelity flag battery (live user report: "my scene came in with no textures").** Root cause isolated empirically via a three-trap e2e scene (each object pins one fidelity risk): a phong file texture rides fine, but **FBX carries only the classic Lambert/Phong material model, so `standardSurface` — the Maya 2020+ default shader family — silently drops its textures**; project-relative texture paths turned out to resolve headless without help (Maya's own fallback resolution — the theorized workspace.mel walker was verified unnecessary and NOT built, though the e2e keeps that trap as a regression pin). Fix lives Maya-side in `templates/_import_scene.py`, exploiting the pull design's key freedom — the conversion scene is throwaway, never saved, so it can be mutated freely: `fbx_safe_materials()` swaps every `standardSurface`/`aiStandardSurface`/`openPBRSurface` shading-group assignment for an equivalent phong — texture connections re-pointed (baseColor→color, emissionColor→incandescence, normalCamera chain as-is), scalars approximated (base-weighted color, specular×weight, roughness→cosinePower, opacity→inverse transparency), attr-name drift across the family handled by a first-readable-attribute probe, and per-material failures degrade to the untranslated shader rather than aborting. The export also now sets the full mayatk-mirrored flag battery (SmoothMesh / Tangents / **Instances** / Skins / Shapes / SkeletonDefinitions / InputConnections, and Cameras + Lights ON — whole-scene import, not asset export), each flag individually tolerated so an FBX plugin version missing one command can't kill the conversion. Verified live end-to-end (`scene_import_live_e2e.py`, now the three-trap textured scene): all textures arrive in Blender 5.1 — the standardSurface trap fails red without the translation and passes green with it. `test_scene_import.py` pins the template contract (+3 checks: translation present, flag battery, per-flag tolerance).

- **2026-07-16 — Macro Manager panel RETIRED — the UI is now the unified uitk `ShortcutEditor` over the `Macros` controller (`Macros.show_editor()`, 1:1 mirror of mayatk's).** Same consolidation as mayatk (see its changelog for the full rationale): the bespoke `edit_utils/macro_manager` panel is gone; `show_editor` launches the shared editor through uitk's new `RegistrySwitchboardFacade` — categories as the group combobox, the header-menu preset row over the same `PresetStore` `apply_saved_macros` applies at startup (essential here: the addon keyconfig doesn't outlive the process), per-row reset to the shipped default. In-UI recategorization dropped by design (the `*Macros` mixin is the category SSoT; presets still carry custom `cat`s). New Qt-free provider glue mirrors mayatk name-for-name (`editor_categories`, `get_editor_registry`, `apply_editor_binding`, `export_bindings`, `import_bindings`); `edit_utils/macro_manager/` remains solely as the shipped-presets anchor; tentacle `preferences.b011` now calls `show_editor`. Tests: `test_blender_ui_handler` 183 (editor-launch checks replace the panel checks), headless `test_macros` 62/62 in a fresh Blender 5.1.
- **2026-07-16 — `FbxUtils.import_fbx` now runs under `window_context_override()` — the import half had the exact context fault the export half already guards.** Pre-existing latent bug surfaced by the new Maya-scene import path (its first Qt-event-pump consumer): `io_scene_fbx` reads context internally on import too (it selects the imported objects), so a bare `bpy.ops.import_scene.fbx` raises when driven from tentacle's Qt pump timer (`bpy.context.window is None`) — the same fault class as the FBX exporter, the RizomUV bridge, and `open_editor` before their wraps. Export in the same file has carried the override (with the explanatory comment) all along; import now matches. No behavior change headless (`--background` has a window; override degrades safely). `test_fbx_utils` / `test_rizom_roundtrip` / `test_scene_exporter` / full suite green.

- **2026-07-16 — New pull-direction Maya bridge: `MayaSceneImport` / `btk.import_maya_scene` — import a Maya scene (`.ma`/`.mb`) via a headless-Maya FBX round-trip.** Reading `.ma` directly was rejected on a structural dealbreaker: a mesh with live construction history stores its *node chain*, not vertex data, so a parser returns empty geometry for most real working scenes. Instead: render the dependency-free `templates/_import_scene.py` (underscore-prefixed → hidden from the bridge panel's template list) → run it under a **fresh `mayapy`** (`pythontk.run_script_to_artifact`; session-safety rule holds — never attaches) → the script opens the scene (`loadReferenceDepth="all"`), loads `fbxmaya`, `FBXResetExport` + explicit `FBXExportHardEdges -v false` (the dense-mesh hang), exports FBX → `FbxUtils.import_fbx` brings it in and returns the new objects → intermediate FBX removed on success / kept + logged on failure (`ptk.TempArtifacts` scoped policy). The template judges success **by the artifact** and exits via `os._exit` (standalone teardown is a known crasher; exit code is advisory). Deliberately NOT a `HandoffBridge` subclass — a pull inverts the push pipeline (input is a path, the payload is produced Maya-side, the caller needs the result); the shared pieces are the `_SPEC.app` AppSpec discovery, the `__KEY__` renderer, and the new pythontk runner. Sibling of `MayaBridge` in `env_utils/maya_bridge/` (`_scene_import.py`); btk-only by design (Maya opens its own scenes natively — ledgered in `tentacle/docs/parity_map.py`; mirror direction `.blend`→Maya is a ~20-line template on the same primitive if ever wanted). Fix caught live and pinned: the install scan returns `maya.EXE`, so mayapy derivation is suffix-case-insensitive, and `require_mayapy` names what's actually missing instead of claiming Maya isn't installed. Verified end-to-end against a real history-bearing `.ma` (extruded cube + sphere, generated by mayapy): fresh Blender 5.1 `--background` imported both meshes with the extrude *evaluated* (16 verts), zero temp leftovers, 4.6s conversion. New `test/test_scene_import.py` (24 checks, venv- and Blender-harness-runnable) covers template hygiene, rendering (compiles as Python), discovery/derivation, validation, and the convert→import→cleanup orchestration with the mayapy run + FBX import stubbed.

- **2026-07-16 — New `Macros.m_grid` (grid-only toggle, bound to `0`) — mirrors the new mayatk macro.** Toggles the floor grid together with its X/Y axis lines (which together are Blender's equivalent of Maya's single grid) and **returns the applied state**. `m_grid_and_image_planes` now delegates to it instead of carrying its own copy of the overlay writes, so the grid-leads/planes-follow order this package already had is expressed once rather than twice (`None` return = no 3D viewport → the caller sits out, as before). No behavior change on this side: blendertk was already the correct half of the pair — mayatk's counterpart called `cmds.grid` from *inside* its image-plane loop and so did nothing in a scene without image planes (fixed; see mayatk's CHANGELOG). Discovery needs no plumbing: the `m_` prefix and the defining `DisplayMacros` mixin give `list_available_macros` / `macro_category` ("Display") the macro for free. Bound to `0` in the shared `m3trik` preset (byte-identical across both DCC stores); `F1` keeps grid+image-planes. Verified in fresh headless Blender 5.1: `test_macros.py` **62/62** (5 new checks — toggle+return, toggle back, planes follow OFF/ON, and the grid still toggling with NO image planes) plus an end-to-end run of the real `apply_saved_macros()` launch path (`0` → `ZERO` → `m_grid` in all three target keymaps: `3D View`, `Object Mode`, `Mesh`; `bpy.ops.btk.macro(macro="m_grid")` → `{'FINISHED'}` with `show_floor` flipping).

- **2026-07-16 — `test_macros.py` no longer wipes the artist's active macro preset (silent, total macro-hotkey loss).** The 12b preset round-trip ran against the **live** `%LOCALAPPDATA%/uitk` user tier, and both halves move the `.active` pointer as a side effect: `save_preset` sets it to the preset it just wrote (`macros.py`: `store.save(...)` then `store.active = name`), and `delete_preset` → `PresetStore.delete` clears a now-dangling pointer (`if self.active == name and not self.exists(name): self.active = None`). So a single suite run left `.active = None` — the file's "cleaned up afterward" comment was true of the *file* but not the pointer. The next launch then resolved `apply_saved_macros`'s documented `name or store.active or DEFAULT_PRESET` chain to the shipped all-unbound `default`, unbinding **every** macro hotkey with nothing logged — the fallback is by design, so the failure is indistinguishable from "no preset configured". Reproduced through the real `Macros` API against a temp store (`save_preset` → `active='_test_roundtrip'`; `delete_preset` → `active=None`), which is exactly the state found on the dev machine: `blendertk .active=None` while `mayatk .active='m3trik'` and the identical `m3trik.json` sat in **both** stores — mayatk survived only because its `test_macros.py` already sandboxes via the `_TempPresetRoot` mixin, which blendertk never mirrored. Fixed by the in-repo flat-script precedent (`test_scene_exporter.py:38`): a per-run `tempfile.mkdtemp` assigned to `UITK_PRESETS_ROOT` before the store is first touched — honored by `pythontk.core_utils.user_config.user_config_root` (and shared *by name* with uitk's `get_presets_root`, so one override moves both stores), `rmtree`'d at the end. Only the **user** tier moves: `builtin_dir` is an absolute repo path, so the shipped-`default` assertions are untouched. New check *"preset store user tier is sandboxed"* asserts `_preset_store().user_dir` resolves under the temp root — it fails the moment anyone drops the sandbox, guarding the whole file rather than just 12b. Verified: `test_macros.py` **57/57** in fresh Blender 5.1 with the live `.active` reading `m3trik` **before and after** the run (pre-fix the same run left it `None`), and full runner **ALL 73 SUITES PASS** with both DCC pointers intact.

- **2026-07-16 — `open_editor`: run the window ops under a context override (live `uv#b031` traceback).** A live *Open UV Editor* click raised `RuntimeError: Operator bpy.ops.wm.window_new.poll() failed, context is incorrect`: `window_new`'s poll reads `context.window`, which is `None` when tentacle drives a slot from its Qt event-pump timer — the same fault `window_context_override` already covers for the FBX exporter and the RizomUV bridge. `open_editor` was the last op in `ui_utils` running bare (every other one already overrides). Both branches (`wm.window_new`, `screen.userpref_show`) now run under `window_context_override()` and honor the documented "returns None when it could not be opened" contract instead of raising when the poll refuses (no window at all, a fullscreen screen, `--background`). Neither branch trusts `windows[-1]` to be what it opened any more: it silently aliased to the MAIN window whenever nothing opened — one CANCELLED poll away from retyping the user's viewport to a UV editor — so the returned window is now identified positively, by pointer diff (`window_new`) or by carrying the editor (`userpref_show` re-focuses an already-open Preferences window instead of appending one — measured, not assumed). An unknown `ui_type` also closes the window it opened to test the name (new private `_close_window`) rather than stranding a duplicate viewport behind the caller's "could not open" message. Verified: `open_editor_gui_check.py` **14/14** in a fresh GUI Blender 5.1 (the no-context-window check reproduces the reported traceback and fails 5/7 without the fix); headless `test_ui_utils.py` 29/29 pins the `--background` → `None` half; full headless suite 73/73.
- **2026-07-16 — Chord menu: Animation → Rigging chain (mirror of Maya's Key → Skeleton) + the previously-unreachable Object submenus.** Live report: the tree jumped straight to *Armature* where Maya starts its branch at the general *Key* (animation) menu, and key Blender menus (Animation, Constraints, Modifiers, Quick Effects) were missing — they are Object-menu **submenus** with no menu-bar entry, unreachable under the old `wm.call_menu` wrap but freely harvestable now. `BlenderNativeMenus` gains `object_animation` / `object_constraints` / `modifiers` / `quick_effects` (compound where the bare word is taken by the shared custom menus or the pose node) and a second mode-adaptive node **`rig`** ("Rigging": the Pose menu in Pose mode, Edit Armature otherwise — out of mode it opens poll-greyed via the edit_object injection); `SELECT_BY_MODE` generalizes into a `MODE_ADAPTIVE` table (Select unchanged). Tree (tentacle `ui/blender_menus`, 22 → 27 files): the startmenu's mid-left slot becomes **Animation** (`object_animation`, exactly Maya's Key slot) → hub \[Animation, Rigging\] → rig hub \[Rigging, Armature, Pose, Constraints\] → pose keeps \[Constraints, IK\]; `armature` becomes a leaf; the `object` node becomes a hub \[Object, Modifiers, Quick Effects\] covering Maya's Deform / Effects domains. Verified: `blender_menus_check.py` **40/40** in fresh headless Blender 5.1 (all four new menus wrap — Animation 9 rows; `rig` resolves per mode and wraps greyed out of mode), offscreen `test_blender_menu_nav.py` 7 tests / 93 subtests against the 27-file tree.

- **2026-07-16 — `m_toggle_panels` now hides the topbar too (mayatk parity), via `btk.toggle_window_bars`.** The macro toggled only the 3D viewport's own regions (header/T/N) — mayatk's counterpart toggles the main **menu bar** (Blender's topbar analogue) *and* the panel bars in sync, so one press left Blender's topbar sitting there. The gap wasn't an oversight: hiding the topbar wasn't expressible from Python until `toggle_window_bars` landed. Now wired, mirroring Maya's structure — the **bars lead** (Maya reads `menuBar().isVisible()` as the shared state; here `toggle_window_bars()`'s return value IS the applied state) and the viewport regions follow it, so bars hidden out-of-band are absorbed on the next press instead of flipping out of phase forever. Signature mirrors mayatk's `toggle_menu=True, toggle_panels=True` opt-outs. Headless and in fullscreen-area mode the bars sit out and the regions lead themselves (prior behavior, so the macro stays useful everywhere). **Three bugs surfaced in live verification, all fixed:** (1) `toggle_window_bars` wasn't bars-only — `use_hide_panels=True` also collapses the *fullscreened area's own* header+toolbar and `back_to_previous` doesn't restore them (probed: the state-matched exit does, `back_to_previous` doesn't), so the round-trip left the viewport's panels collapsed as a side effect; it now snapshots/restores them, and the check asserts the flags rather than just geometry (which is blind to this — region flags don't move area rects, which is how it slipped through the first review). (2) The macro captured `space` *before* the round-trip that rebuilds the screen, so writes through the stale ref silently vanished. (3) Restoring a snapshot verbatim flipped an *untouched* `show_region_ui` back on — every assignment fires a full `ED_area_init`, so the restore now diffs and writes only what changed. Also measured and documented rather than papered over: **`show_region_*` setters silently no-op inside a `temp_override` on 5.1** (the value reads back unchanged from within it), so these are assigned bare and guarded on `bpy.context.window` instead — an override here is safe but useless, and briefly made the macro a no-op. And `toggle_window_bars` is now **all-or-nothing**: the restore assigns bare, which needs a context window, so without one (the Qt event-pump timer state this package's macros are documented to run in) the bars hid while the viewport's regions stayed collapsed — *and it returned a clean success*. It now returns `None` up front rather than half-applying the UI, so such a caller degrades to the regions-only behavior instead of a mangled viewport. The macro's own region half gained the matching guard for a **pre-existing latent crash** it inherited: assigning `show_region_*` bare fires `ED_area_init`, which dereferences the context window, so windowless it was a hard access violation — and a crash costs the user their unsaved scene, so it now sits out instead (a keymap-invoked macro always has a window, so the normal path is unchanged). Verified in fresh Blender 5.1 GUI: `fullscreen_area_gui_check.py` **37/37** (adds bars-only-contract flag assertions, one-press sync both ways, drift absorption, both opt-out halves, and the windowless all-or-nothing guard under a forced `temp_override(window=None)`) + `test_macros.py` **56/56** / `test_ui_utils.py` **27/27** headless.

- **2026-07-16 — New `btk.toggle_window_bars`: hide the main window's topbar (File/Edit/Render… menus + workspace tabs) + statusbar while KEEPING the tiled layout; new `btk.toggle_fullscreen_area` (Ctrl+Alt+Space / Ctrl+Space as helpers).** Blender exposes no switch for the topbar — it's a *global area*: absent from `screen.areas`, no `SpaceTopBar` RNA type, no `show_topbar` flag (all probed on 5.1.2, headless + live GUI) — so "hide that bar" looked impossible without giving up the layout. Probing the two exits of fullscreen-area mode found the seam: entering `SCREENFULL` hides the global bars, and exiting via `screen.back_to_previous` restores the tiled layout while **leaving them hidden**, whereas the state-matched `screen_full_area` exit un-hides them. `toggle_window_bars(visible=None)` round-trips through fullscreen without an intervening redraw (no visible flash): the areas expand into the reclaimed ~26 px row, the hidden state survives workspace switches, and showing again restores the exact pre-hide layout. Since the bars have no RNA presence, visibility is *measured geometrically* (`_window_bars_hidden`: does the topmost area reach the window top?) — so the return value is read back, not assumed, and a future Blender changing the `back_to_previous` behavior degrades to a truthful no-op instead of lying. Caveats pinned by probe: the two bars ride together (`show_statusbar` can't resurrect the statusbar while the topbar is hidden), and the helper returns None in fullscreen-area mode (which manages the bars itself). `toggle_fullscreen_area(editor=None, hide_panels=True)` wraps the underlying native mode as a toggle (largest-area default; `hide_panels=False` = Ctrl+Space maximize) — its exit must re-pass the entered state's props (`screen_full_area` toggles per-state; a mismatched call is a silent no-op, probed in both directions), and the state is only *guessable* geometrically: a maximize entered while the bars are hidden covers the full window, indistinguishable from `SCREENFULL`, and a single measured-guess exit stranded the window in fullscreen (reproduced live as a failing check first, per TDD) — so the restore branch tries the guess and, still fullscreen, safely retries the other prop. mayatk has no counterpart (Maya chrome toggles are one-liners) — same rationale as `toggle_editor`. Verified live in fresh Blender 5.1 GUI: new `test/fullscreen_area_gui_check.py` **25/25** (constraint pin, full-window coverage on/off with exact layout restore, named-editor targeting, maximize keeps bars, bars hide/show round-trip byte-exact, idempotent targeted calls, fullscreen-mode composition, and the bars-hidden+maximize combo exiting cleanly) + headless guards in `test_ui_utils.py` (27/27); full runner ALL 73 SUITES PASS.

- **2026-07-16 — Target Weld: the Blender build of Maya's interactive Merge Vertex Tool (`btk.target_weld` / `TargetWeld`).** Blender has no native drag-a-vertex-onto-another weld (vertex-snap + auto-merge only approximates the workflow), so `edit_utils/target_weld.py` ships a modal operator mirroring Maya's `targetWeldCtx`/`MergeVertexTool` functionally and cosmetically: a persistent tool (repeated welds until Esc/RMB; Esc mid-drag cancels the drag only), press-drag-release gesture with a dashed rubber band, hovered candidate in Maya's preselection aqua, armed source in selected-component yellow, a Maya-`inViewMessage`-style top-center prompt, crosshair cursor, and one undo step per weld so Ctrl+Z steps weld-by-weld. `merge_to_center=False` lands the merged vert at the target (Maya Target Weld), `True` at the midpoint (Maya's Weld Center flavor of the same context). Picking is screen-space like Maya's — every visible vert projected per mouse-move with one numpy matmul, front-most within a 14 px radius wins — so border/isolated verts pick without BVH/ray-cast; per-object caches rebuild on BMesh invalidation (undo, weld, pass-through topology ops) and on every press. Activation mirrors the Maya slot's prep (Edit mode, vertex mask, deselect) under a VIEW_3D override so it launches from the Qt marking-menu timer context; multi-object Edit Mode picks across all meshes but enforces same-mesh pairs (Maya can't express the cross-object case — refused with a prompt). The operator registers lazily (`_ensure_operator`, the `macros.py` pattern) and `bpy` is guarded, so the package surface resolves DCC-free; `--background` activation refuses with a deterministic `RuntimeError` (a modal invoke there is an "Invalid operator call" that CANCELs silently — and background layouts DO contain a VIEW_3D area, so the guard keys off `bpy.app.background`, not the area scan). Backs tentacle's Blender `polygons.b043`/`b008` (see tentacle's CHANGELOG). **Navigation is never consumed** (live-repro fix: the first hands-on session lost tumbling/panning): plain MMB / wheel / trackpad / NDOF always passed through, but the Maya-style keymaps (Industry Compatible / Alt-nav / emulated 3-button) bind navigation to **Alt+mouse** — the tool consumed Alt+LMB (tumble dead) and treated Alt+RMB as its exit gesture (dolly closed the tool). Every Alt-modified event now passes through before the LMB/RMB handling, matching Maya's own tool contexts where Alt tumble/track/dolly stay live; Alt also abandons an in-flight drag (its passed-through LMB release would otherwise leave a ghost rubber band armed with no button held — Maya likewise hands the gesture to the camera), and any navigation drops the hover highlight (its cached screen position is stale the moment the view moves; the armed source keeps re-projecting). Verified headless in fresh Blender 5.1: `test_target_weld.py` **33/33** (projection/pick/dash/merge-position math, edge-connected + unconnected BMesh welds at target and center, hidden-vert pick-cache exclusion + cache reuse, idempotent registration, background guard, and the modal navigation contract — Alt+LMB/Alt+RMB/MMB/wheel pass through, Alt abandons the drag, MMB keeps the source but drops the hover, plain Esc still exits); the modal invoke path (timer-context activation, live modal handler, draw handler surviving real redraws) is proven by the self-quitting windowed check `test/target_weld_gui_check.py` (4/4 in a fresh GUI Blender); the drag gesture itself is exercised by hand.

- **2026-07-16 — Chord menu reaches FULL Maya wrap parity: native menus are harvested into Qt and hosted in a pinnable Switchboard window (pin header + hide on `key_show` release); the `wm.call_menu` popup path — and the interim "menu unavailable" hint gate — are gone.** Live report: releasing a chord node popped Blender's self-managing popup (no pin header, no hide on `key_show`), and the interim mode-gate replaced out-of-mode menus with hint spam. Blender's GL popups can't be Qt-hosted (no HWND, no `QAction`s) — but every Blender menu **is a Python class**, so new `ui_utils/menu_harvest.py` executes the menu's `draw(self, context)` against a duck-typed recorder layout and rebuilds the recording as `QAction`s: operators fire deferred `bpy.ops` invokes (`INVOKE_DEFAULT` under a VIEW_3D override, one tick later; errors surface via `popup_message`), `operator_menu_enum`/`operator_enum` expand from RNA enum items, boolean `layout.prop` rows become checkable actions, nested menus recurse (path-scoped cycle guard + depth cap; a failing child draw degrades to one disabled row), and every row is `poll()`-greyed exactly like Blender's own. Sub-layouts (`row`/`column`/…) share the recorder's item list with `enabled=False` inheritance; unknown layout members (`template_*`, popovers) swallow via a `_Sink` fallback — asset-catalog entries/icons/shortcut hints are the accepted fidelity gaps. Harvest + polls run under one `get_view3d_context` override so mode-sensitive draws and polls see a real menu-open context. `BlenderNativeMenus` gains the instance API mirroring `MayaNativeMenus` (`get_menu` → `uitk.EmbeddedMenuWidget`; widgets cached for window identity, **content re-harvested every show** so it tracks the live mode and add-ons); `BlenderUiHandler.show` presents it via new `_wrap_native_menu`, which populates the pre-registered blank proxy **in place** (`MainWindow.setCentralWidget` + Maya's header/style pipeline + `fit_to_window`) — the window's identity never changes, deliberately: the marking menu's `_show_window` raises/activates the object it resolved from `loaded_ui` on a deferred timer, so a swapped-in window would leave that timer targeting the dead proxy. Pin/hide-on-`key_show` behavior is the shared marking-menu window path with no special casing. Out of mode, everything still opens (Maya's menus are always openable): menus whose draw survives show poll-greyed, and for the two draws that hard-deref `context.edit_object` (`VIEW3D_MT_edit_armature`, `VIEW3D_MT_edit_curve_ctrlpoints` — the reported `space_view3d.py` AttributeError) the harvest injects the **active object** as `edit_object` (any object satisfies the deref; rows come out greyed), falling back to the hand-authored `<name>#submenu` overlay only when no active object exists. Live "dead buttons" report (armature / ctrl_points / vertex / edge) fixed by that injection plus **nested macro-operator props**: the edit-mesh Vertices/Edges draws configure macros via nested groups (`props.MESH_OT_rip.use_fill = False`) — `_OpProps` now records unset-attribute reads as nested recorders (serialized to the nested-dict `bpy.ops` macro form) instead of raising, which had killed those harvests in *every* mode. The interim (unreleased) `REQUIRED_MODES`/`mode_hint` gate is deleted; `popup_message` stays (generic + the op-error surface); `call_native_menu` remains the transient at-cursor popup one-liner. `PersistentMenu`/`EmbeddedMenuWidget` moved to **uitk** (`widgets/embeddedMenu.py`, pure Qt — shared home; mayatk re-exports). Verified in fresh headless Blender 5.1: rewritten `tentacle/test/blender/blender_menus_check.py` **27/27** — the wrap returns a headered, `blender_menu`-tagged `MainWindow` (18-row Add; 31-row Object incl. nested submenus and live rows), window identity is cached across re-wraps with refreshed content, armature degrades out of mode and wraps in its mode (19 rows; Pose 20), a triggered row schedules exactly one deferred invoke, `show(proxy)` routes through the wrap, and Select resolves per live mode.

- **2026-07-16 — Script Output capture: log levels ride along with each chunk, ANSI escapes scrubbed at ingest.** Both fixes trace to the tee. `_StreamTee.__getattr__` delegates `isatty()` to Blender's system console — a real TTY — so CPython emits its *colored* traceback and the escape bytes landed in the transcript, where the Qt view rendered them as literal `[35m` garbage (the reported symptom: a `space_view3d.py` draw-handler `AttributeError` arriving as `[1;35mAttributeError[0m`). `_write` now scrubs via `ptk.strip_ansi` at ingest, so the buffer holds plain text and its char cap counts characters a reader will actually see. Separately, past the tee a logging record is just characters — this is the last layer that still knows what it *was* — so `_write(text, level=None)` buffers `(text, level)` pairs, `_SinkHandler` forwards `record.levelno`, and the listener/`_append` hand it to `ScriptOutput.append_text`, which colors by level rather than by re-sniffing words (a DEBUG line mentioning "error" is not an error). Plain `stdout`/`stderr` writes carry no level and fall back to the word/block rules. `chunks()` replaces `text()` as the buffer's one accessor and returns the level-tagged pairs; `_ensure_widget` seeds from it chunk-by-chunk rather than one joined string, so a rebuilt console recolors history exactly as it was colored live (measured: ~99ms one-time for a transcript at the 200k cap). Tracebacks themselves now color as one region — see uitk's `ScriptBlockRule`. New `test/test_script_output.py` (12 checks; needs neither Qt nor bpy, so it runs under the Blender harness and the workspace `.venv` alike).

- **2026-07-16 — New `ui_utils.qt_dock.QtDock`: dock ANY Qt widget into a native Blender area as a true child window — the Script Output console rebuilt on it, overlay machinery deleted.** The console's overlay design (`_DockAnchor` + `_SkinShadow`: a frameless top-level Qt window shadow-tracked over a docked decoy area by a 50 ms `bpy` timer doing screen-coordinate/DPI math, OS-owner juggling and minimize polling) is replaced by a purpose-built, reusable container. **Mechanism (probe-verified in fresh Blender 5.1 before productizing):** the Qt widget's native window becomes a real **`WS_CHILD` of the GHOST window** (`QtGui.QWindow.fromWinId` foreign-window parenting) glued to a docked area's WINDOW region — `WS_CLIPCHILDREN` on the GHOST window keeps Blender's GL present clipped around the embedded surface (the classic embed-into-a-GL-app arrangement; pixel-verified: the Qt surface survives Blender redraws). A child window moves/minimizes/stacks/clips with Blender **natively**, so the timer, the y-flip-to-screen transform, the `/dpr` divide (child coords are parent-client physical px on both sides), `set_owner` re-assertion and `is_iconic` polling all ceased to exist. Geometry sync is **event-driven**: a draw handler on the area's space type re-positions the child exactly when the region redraws (area-border drags, window resizes, DPI changes) — verified tracking an OS-window resize at 0 px delta; a cheap 0.5 s liveness watchdog covers the two silences a draw handler can't report (area closed/merged away, host window gone) and detaches via `on_detach`. One crash found and fixed en route: setting `space.show_region_header` from a bare `bpy` timer hard-crashes (`ED_area_init` → `WM_window_get_active_workspace` on a NULL context window, EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION) — `QtDock` applies it under a full `temp_override`. **`script_output.py` shrinks to two collaborators:** the unchanged pure-Python `_OutputCapture` (startup tee → transcript buffer) and a `QtDock` hosting the same shared `uitk.ScriptOutput` widget (identical font/highlighting/context menu as mayatk's console — cosmetic 1:1 by construction); module API (`show`/`toggle`/`hide`/`begin_capture`/`restore`) unchanged. New: `hide()` persists the user's **strip height** alongside the visible flag, so a resized console re-docks at its adjusted height next show and next session (Maya keeps a `workspaceControl`'s size the same way); a corrupt state file falls back to defaults. The no-Qt / off-Windows degrade (bare docked native Info Log, reason logged) is preserved. `BlenderWindow` sheds the overlay-only members (`region_screen_rect`, `is_iconic`, `new_ghost_hwnd`) and gains the child-embed primitives (`set_clip_children`, `region_client_rect`, `move_child`); `set_owner` stays — it's the hosting mode for *standalone* owned panels. Verified end-to-end in fresh Blender 5.1: rewritten `tentacle/test/blender/console_dock_check.py` **30/30** (production startup order, WS_CHILD parentage, 0 px region fit, print/logging/error-coloring, hide/show history, height persistence, reload `teardown()`, cold cross-session `restore()`) and `editors_toggle_check.py` **24/24** through the real b010 slot — both with clean exits.

- **2026-07-16 — `QtDock`: keyboard focus follows the mouse, so a docked Qt panel's shortcuts work on hover (Ctrl+C etc.) and Blender gets its keys back on leave.** An embedded child window is a real OS focus target, so the console only received keys after a **click**, and then never gave them back — Blender stayed keyboard-dead until the user clicked a Blender region. New `focus_on_hover` (default True) syncs win32 focus to "is the pointer over the widget?", composing with the widget's Qt-level focus (uitk's `ScriptOutput.focus_on_hover` — see uitk's CHANGELOG): win32 focus routes native key messages to our child window, Qt focus routes them to the right widget inside it. The HWND half deliberately stays out of the widget so any plain QWidget can dock here and behave the same. **This one is a poll (20 Hz, only while docked) — and the measurement is why**: Qt's `Enter` does fire for the embedded child, but `Leave` **never** does (a child inside a GL-painted foreign parent; the harness measured `enters=1, leaves=0`), so releasing on Leave would strand focus and leave Blender dead — strictly worse than before. Polling both directions is one self-correcting mechanism instead of two half-working ones, and it also recovers from teleporting pointers (alt-tab, window switches) that no crossing event reports. Blender's own focus-follows-mouse is loop-driven for exactly the same reason. Guarded against the two states the cursor position lies about: an open Qt popup (our own context menu is a separate top-level — the cursor reads as "not over the widget" and we'd yank focus out from under the menu) and a held mouse button (a drag-select straying outside the widget still belongs to it); it is inert by construction when another of our Qt windows holds focus (`GetFocus() != child`), and `SetFocus` is thread-scoped so it can never steal keys from another application. Undock/detach stops the poll and hands focus back **before** un-parenting, so hiding while hovered can't strand focus on a hidden window. New `BlenderWindow` primitives: `set_keyboard_focus` / `keyboard_focus` / `cursor_over` (focus ≠ foreground — tentacle's `_NativeWindow` only ever managed the latter, so these are not duplicates). Verified in fresh Blender 5.1 with the **real** cursor and **real** keystrokes (a synthetic Qt event would fake the very routing under test): `console_dock_check.py` **33/33** — hovering hands the console OS focus with no click, a real Ctrl+C copies its text, and moving back over the viewport returns focus to Blender; `editors_toggle_check.py` 24/24.

- **2026-07-16 — `test_curve_to_tube.py` un-broken: the slot-phase stub was stale.** The LightmapBaker-parity commit (138e18e) replaced the Quads checkbox (`chk002`) with the `cmb_topology` Quads/Triangles combo in the `.ui` + slot but never updated the test's stub UI, so `perform_operation` raised `AttributeError: 'U' object has no attribute 'cmb_topology'` and the suite failed 19/20 (the only red suite in the full run). Stub now provides `cmb_topology` (currentData `"quads"`) and drops the dead `chk002`; suite 21/21.

- **2026-07-15 — Script Output overhauled: decomposed design, capture-from-startup, cross-session persistence.** The console module (`env_utils/script_output.py`) is re-architected from one monolithic class into three focused collaborators behind a thin `ScriptConsole` orchestrator: `_OutputCapture` (pure-Python stdout/stderr tee + root logging handler → a bounded transcript buffer + live listener — no Qt, no bpy, so it can install before any UI exists), `_DockAnchor` (the native Info Log area docked in the main window), and `_SkinShadow` (the frameless `uitk.ScriptOutput` overlay + geometry tick, **seeded from the transcript buffer** when built). Two user-facing wins: **(1) capture starts at startup, not at first open** — new module function `begin_capture()` is called by the Blender startup script before `import tentacle`, so the greeting banner and every early print are in the console whenever it's first shown (fixes the live "the banner and tool feedback never appear" report at the root: previously the redirect only installed when the skin was first built). **(2) The shown/hidden state persists across Blender sessions** — `show()`/`hide()` record a `visible` flag in `<Blender user config>/blendertk_script_output.json`, and the new `restore()` (called by `TclBlender.__init__` at launch — the Blender analogue of Maya's `workspaceControl` `uiScript` restore) re-opens the console if it was open when the previous session ended. Runtime self-heal paths (main window/area vanishing mid-tick) deliberately do NOT write the flag, so a quit-time tick can't cancel the next session's restore; tests sandbox the flag via `ScriptConsole._state_dir_override` (the `ShotStore._prefs_dir_override` pattern). The quit-time atexit guard is stronger than before: it detaches the skin listener FIRST and only unwinds `sys.stdout`/`stderr` when they are still ours (a foreign redirect stacked on top of the tee survives). A host reload (`tb.reload()`) is handled too: new `ScriptConsole.teardown()` un-installs everything EXCEPT the persisted flag (undock + skin/timer/listener drop + stream restore) — without it the old module's persistent tick, skin, tee and docked area survive `ptk.reload_package` and the reloaded module's `restore()` docks a SECOND Info Log area; `BlenderHost.reload` calls it on the old module before reloading. Verified in fresh Blender 5.1: `console_shadow_check.py` 29/29 — the full production order (pre-import banner capture → `restore()` no-op with no saved state → show/hide flag round-trip → history across hide/show → a simulated reload `teardown()` (streams restored, flag kept) + next-session cold `restore()` that re-opens the console by itself) — and `editors_toggle_check.py` 23/23 through the real b010 slot, both with clean process exits. (Harness fix along the way: `wm.quit_blender` from a bpy timer crashes in `wm_exit_schedule_delayed` when the context window resolves NULL — e.g. after an OS-owned overlay held focus — so the checks now quit under an explicit window `temp_override`.)

- **2026-07-15 — Editors ▸ Time & Range (`b009`) toggles the docked Timeline in place (Maya parity); new `btk.toggle_editor`.** The Blender editors menu routed every button through `open_editor` → an unconditional `wm.window_new()`, so Time & Range spawned a *new* Timeline window each click instead of mirroring Maya's `ToggleTimeSlider` (hide/show the docked strip). New composable `ui_utils` primitives — `find_editor` (matches on `area.ui_type`, since a Timeline is `area.type=="DOPESHEET_EDITOR"` and only `ui_type` tells it apart from a Dope Sheet), `close_editor` (`screen.area_close`), `dock_editor` (split the tallest `VIEW_3D`, switch the new bottom strip's `ui_type`), and `toggle_editor` (close-if-shown else dock) — give a *docked* toggle that never floats a window. Stateless: re-docks at the bottom of the viewport, like Maya's always-bottom time slider. mayatk has no `toggle_editor` (Maya toggles chrome with one `mel.eval`); blendertk needs the helper because a Blender area toggle is multi-step — the same reason `open_editor` exists as a helper. Empirically grounded first (a fresh-Blender area probe: close + targeted-split restore is clean, **zero ghost areas** — the generic-`area_split` fragility the console-shadow work hit does NOT reproduce for a targeted split), then verified end-to-end through the real slot (`tentacle/test/blender/editors_toggle_check.py`, fresh Blender 5.1, 7/7): click hides in place → click re-docks full-viewport-width at the bottom → no floating window ever spawns.

- **2026-07-15 — Script Output is now a TRUE dock in the main window, with persistent capture across hide/show (was: a live "opens but looks empty" report).** Two compounding problems: (1) the Info Log anchor was a *separate* `wm.window_new()` window, not a real dock — the anchor method (renamed `_open_anchor` → `_dock_anchor`) now calls the new `btk.dock_editor("Info Log")` (the same primitive b009's Timeline toggle uses) to split it into the MAIN window instead, resolved via a new `BlenderWindow.window_hwnd(bpy_window)` (match a *specific* window by live client size — empirically confirmed non-ambiguous: a `wm.window_new()`-spawned editor window does NOT clone the main window's exact size on this Blender build) rather than the old spawn-diffing `new_ghost_hwnd`. (2) `hide()`/`show()` fully tore down the skin + stdout/stderr redirect every cycle, so reopening always started from an empty pane even though the system console (and Blender itself) had been chattering the whole time — the skin + redirect now build ONCE and persist for the Blender session; `hide()` only undocks the area + hides the (still-alive) skin, so a later `show()` picks up with all history intact (mirrors Maya's Script Editor, which has recorded continuously since Maya started). Two bugs surfaced fixing this, both fixed: persisting the redirect forever meant nothing ever restored `sys.stdout`/`stderr` before Blender's own quit-time teardown — `_StreamTee.write()` firing against a partially-destroyed Qt skin during interpreter shutdown reproduced as a hard crash (exit code 11) in `console_shadow_check.py`; fixed via `atexit.register(self._remove_redirect)` the first time the redirect installs. And `hide()` originally undocked via the by-`ui_type` `close_editor` (closes *every* Info Log area in the window) — switched to a new single-area `btk.close_area(window, area)` primitive (which `close_editor` now composes, DRY) so hiding can't take out a user's own separately-opened Info Log area. Verified end-to-end through the real slot in fresh Blender 5.1: `editors_toggle_check.py` 21/21 (dock/undock via true area ops, no new `bpy.Window`, skin shadows pixel-exact, and the actual fix — a marker printed before hide is still there after a hide→show round-trip, plus one printed while hidden also appears) and `console_shadow_check.py` 18/18 (adds the same persistence round-trip to the OS-ownership/logging/color-highlight coverage), both with a clean process exit.

- **2026-07-15 — RizomUV bridge round-trip ported (Blender) — `pack` / `unwrap_hard` / `unwrap_organic` / `optimize` now run.** The four preset combo items were previously listed for item-count parity but disabled at runtime (`RizomBridgeSlots._disable_round_trip_presets`) because only the one-way `send` had an engine. `RizomUVBridge.process_with_rizomuv` now implements the full round-trip mirroring mayatk: export `__RZTMP`-suffixed **copies** to a temp FBX → run the chosen Lua preset headlessly (`Rizomuv_VS.exe -cfi`) → re-import the RizomUV-written FBX → transfer the new UVs back onto the originals → remove every temp object. Divergences from the Maya path, by construction: **copies not namespaced duplicates** (Blender's FBX import never overwrites — copies are removed before re-import so imports return under the exact `__RZTMP` names the mapping keys on), and a **direct per-loop UV copy** (bulk `foreach_get`/`foreach_set`) instead of `transferAttributes` (RizomUV rewrites only UVs, so the re-import is topologically identical to the original — exact and context-free in the windowless Qt-timer state; a spatial `data_transfer` is the fallback if the loop count ever diverges). The copies export with `use_mesh_modifiers=False` so a mesh with a Subsurf/Mirror modifier round-trips its **base** topology (not the evaluated cage) and the UV transfer stays exact, matching mayatk's base-poly FBX export; and the cleanup purges the material/image datablocks the FBX re-import creates (diffed against a pre-import snapshot) so repeated runs don't accumulate orphan `*.001` datablocks in the .blend. The Lua assets (`scripts/{pack,unwrap_hard,unwrap_organic,optimize}.lua` + `templates/wrapper.lua`) are **vendored byte-identical** from mayatk (pure RizomUV Lua, DCC-agnostic), and the DCC-agnostic Python (version parsing, wrapper substitution, RizomUV-version gating via `parameters.strip_unsupported`/`MIN_VERSIONS`, the headless run + FBX-modified verification) mirrors mayatk's engine. `parameters.PARAMS` regains the ~15 `ZomPack`/`ZomUnfold`/`ZomOptimize` knobs (pulled back verbatim from mayatk); the slot's `_VersionedParamsProxy` hides knobs gated above the installed Rizom (2020.1 access-violates on newer `ZomPack` fields). Slots drop the disabling machinery — `list_template_modes` globs `scripts/*.lua`, `b000` routes non-`send` presets through `process_with_rizomuv`. **Parity**: the rizom panel is now mechanically 1:1 (`compare_panel_surface.py --panel rizom_bridge`: 0 untriaged / 0 pending / 0 item deltas) — the stale `cmb000` `na` ledger entry is retired. **Verified**: `test_rizom_roundtrip.py` (fresh Blender 5.1, RizomUV stubbed) 16/16 — identity round-trip preserves UVs at max-diff 0.00 (loop order survives the FBX round-trip), a simulated UV delta propagates back, two distinct meshes don't cross-wire, a materialed mesh leaves no orphan material/image datablocks, a Subsurf-modified mesh transfers exactly onto its base (24 loops, not the evaluated cage), and every temp object is cleaned up; `test_rizom_construction.py` (venv) 17/17 — preset resolution, wrapper substitution, and 2020.1-vs-2022 version gating. Note: the round-trip engine imports its Qt-backed `parameters` lazily inside the method (import-time Qt-free, matching the marmoset bridge), so it runs in tentacle's live Blender but not a bare `--background` session without a Qt binding — the same accepted limitation as the other bridges.

- **2026-07-14 — Substance/Marmoset bridge "no selected objects" fixed in the windowless (Qt-timer) context.** Clicking *Send to Painter/Toolbag* from the bridge panel aborted with "Nothing selected to export." even with a valid selection AND output dir. Root cause was in `FbxUtils.export`: it read the selection (and the io_scene_fbx exporter reads it *internally*) via `bpy.context.selected_objects`, which raises `AttributeError` when `bpy.context.window is None` — exactly the state tentacle's `bpy.app.timers`/Qt event pump runs the slots in. `FbxUtils.export` now reads the selection window-independently (`btk.selected_objects`) and runs the `select_all` + `export_scene.fbx` operators under a new `btk.window_context_override()` (supplies the first open window so the operator's screen-context reads resolve). Fixes every bridge that hands off through `export_selection_fbx` (Substance, Marmoset, RizomUV). New `core_utils` helper `window_context_override` (the window-only companion to `get_view3d_context`). Verified live on Blender 5.1 under a forced `temp_override(window=None)`; permanent regression test in `test_bridges.py`.

- **2026-07-14 — Bridge FBX export: translate Maya MEL FBX option names to Blender kwargs.** The Substance/Marmoset templates are vendored verbatim from mayatk and carry Maya MEL names (e.g. `FBXExportEmbeddedTextures`, valid in Maya via `mel.eval FBXExport*`); passed straight to `bpy.ops.export_scene.fbx` they faulted with `keyword "FBXExport…" unrecognized` (surfaced once the windowless-export fix let the export run at all). `FbxUtils.export` now runs a `_translate_fbx_options` pass (the Blender side of the "engine does the idiomatic-per-DCC translation" contract): known Maya names map to their Blender kwarg (`FBXExportEmbeddedTextures`→`embed_textures`/`path_mode="COPY"`), unmapped `FBXExport*` (Maya-only) are dropped, and Blender-native keys pass through so a typo'd real kwarg still errors loudly. Templates stay verbatim. Regression tests in `test_bridges.py`.

- **2026-07-14 — Substance/Marmoset bridge no longer requires an Output Dir (temp-folder fallback).** When neither a typed value nor the `.blend`/workspace default resolves (an **unsaved** file), the panel now falls back to a self-cleaning temp folder instead of erroring — mirroring the Maya bridges (`TEMP_OUTPUT_FALLBACK` opt-in on the shared `uitk.BridgeSlotsBase`; the temp dir is registered for cleanup at process exit). Unity's bridge keeps the hard error (its Output Dir must be a real Unity project). Marmoset/Substance help text updated to drop the stale "(required)".

- **2026-07-13 — LightmapBaker: "Atlas by Material" packing ported (mayatk parity)** — `LightmapBaker.pack_atlas` consolidates a lighting-only bake's per-object maps into one shared, area-weighted EXR per primary material and repacks each object's lightmap UVs into its rect, so the exported mesh samples the atlas directly through UV2 (no engine scaleOffset binding). Groups by dominant `material_index`; the DCC-agnostic layout math is **reused** from pythontk (`ptk.ImgUtils.compute_atlas_layout` / `inset_atlas_rects` / `atlas_pixel_rects` — pure-Python, no cv2, the same helpers mayatk uses); the EXR assembly is bpy-native (image load/scale + a numpy paste + gutter-dilate, since Blender's runtime ships no cv2). The applied rect rides the marker's `uvRect`; `revert_lightmap` now inverts it (re-bake starts from the 0-1 layout). The cmb002 "Atlas by Material" item is enabled (the old `_disable_unsupported_packing_mode` removed) and the slot's bake commits via `commit_lightmap(uv_rects=…)`. Verified headlessly (`test_lightmap_baker.py`): 2 objects sharing a material → 1 shared EXR, source maps consolidated, UVs repacked into their rects, revert exactly restores the 0-1 layout (38/38).

- **2026-07-13 — Selection: UV-domain Convert-To + Back/Front-Facing leaves ported (mayatk parity)** — `Selection.select_uv_shell_border` / `select_uv_perimeter` / `select_uv_edge_loop` (cmb003 "Convert To" UV items) are real bmesh UV-graph helpers keyed off a single UV-boundary test (a mesh-open edge or a UV seam where an edge's two faces assign different UVs to a shared vert). The `_SELECTION_CONFIG` UV category gains `Back-Facing` / `Front-Facing` (object-level signed-UV-area winding, matching the sibling Texture Borders / Unmapped leaves). Verified headlessly (`test_selection.py`) on a smart-projected cube (all-seam) and a seamless grid, incl. UV Edge Loop truncating a loop at a real re-unwrapped seam (uv 3 vs native 6).

- **2026-07-12 — Channels: the single/multi-object toggles are color-coded from session start and can no longer drift apart** (mirror of mayatk, same date). The root "state icons start theme-colored until first clicked" bug was upstream (uitk's first theme sweep wiped explicitly-colored state icons — see uitk's CHANGELOG); the option-box actions here benefit with no code change. The compact-footer target button is rewired onto the centralized mechanism: same state dicts as the txt001 option-box action (shared `_target_toggle_states()`) passed to `footer.add_action_button(states=...)` — color-coded and mode-correct at creation; the manual `_update_compact_btn_state` re-tint dance and `_on_footer_compact_btn_clicked` are deleted (this panel's extra startup/compact-toggle re-tint calls existed precisely to paper over the missing initial sync). `_set_single_object` now syncs both toggle widgets to the controller via `_sync_target_toggles()` (the option-box action is kept as `self._target_action`); leaving compact routes through `_set_single_object(False)` instead of resetting the controller behind the widgets' backs. `compare_panel_surface.py --panel channels`: 0 untriaged, summary unchanged.

- **2026-07-12 — Channels: *Compact View* and *Auto-fit Window* now default ON** (mirror of mayatk). Both header-menu checkboxes ship checked. Auto-fit is pull-based (read on every refresh); compact view is push-only, and state-restore only re-emits `toggled` when the stored value differs from the default, so first launch would show the box checked but the effect unapplied. Added `_sync_compact_default()` (Type-column-style pull reconcile) called from `header_init` and the top of `_refresh_table`. Previously-toggled preferences are preserved by restore. `compare_panel_surface.py --panel channels`: 0 deltas.

- **2026-07-12 — Shots-stack review fixes: scene-swap store invalidation (CRITICAL), manifest behavior guards, sequencer edit-scoping, tube-rig b004 end-control routing.** A full review of the shots/tube-rig/UV changeset surfaced one critical and several correctness bugs — all fixed with fail-first repros + pinning tests:
  - **Scene-swap invalidation now actually fires** (critical). `BlenderScenePersistence` gains Maya-parity scene wiring (`_install_scene_jobs`/`remove_callbacks`/`_on_scene_changed` via the `@persistent` `ScriptJobManager` `load_post` master): the three panels' `add_invalidation_listener` registrations had NO producer — nothing called `_notify_invalidated()`/nulled `_active` on File ▸ New/Open, so an open sequencer's non-persistent `bpy.app` handlers died for the session and, worse, the previous file's still-active store wrote its OLD shots JSON into the NEW scene's `shot_store` property on the next save (cross-scene corruption; repro'd pre-fix: stale_active/listener_dead/leaked all true). `pythontk.ShotStore.clear_active()` tears the backend's job down via the new `remove_callbacks`. Lifecycle pinned in `test_shots_adapter.py` (+7: real `wm.read_homefile` — listener fires once, store nulled, fresh store loads, no leak, teardown drops the job).
  - **Manifest behavior application mirrors mayatk's `apply_to_shots` guards** (`_shot_manifest.py`): locked shots are never modified (the panel help's promise), zero-duration shots skip, an object with existing keys in range is skipped (rebuilds are idempotent — never overwrite user animation), an already-placed VSE strip is never re-added (every Build previously stacked `name.001` duplicates; `reapply_object` also placed one strip PER behavior name), and `_verify_behavior` routes `verify.mode: audio_clip` templates to the strip check (`_audio_exists`) — built audio steps previously assessed `missing_behavior` forever because strip names were looked up in `bpy.data.objects`. Returns mayatk-shaped `{"applied","skipped","failed"}` records with per-entry try/except (a failing entry no longer aborts the batch mid-build; the footer's `failed` tally is real now, and its skip label reads "already keyed/placed"). Dead `gap_threshold` parameter threading removed (`store.detect_regions()` owns the threshold). `test_shot_manifest.py` 24/24 (+7: audio idempotency/verify, locked/zero-duration/existing-keys guards, failed bucket).
  - **Sequencer edit-scoping + handler hygiene** (`shot_sequencer/`): `on_keys_moved` is two-pass per fcurve (chained batch moves like `[(10,12),(12,14)]` stacked both keys on 14); whole-object "Delete Key" scopes to the clip's TRANSFORM fcurves (custom-prop/constraint/modifier curves survive); quaternion channels get W-first, euler-distinct labels (`quatRotate[WXYZ]` — the X-first shared table mislabeled every quat channel by one axis and label collisions made sub-row edits drag both rotation families); `depsgraph_update_post` is scoped to real keyframe edits via an `Action`-in-`depsgraph.updates` filter + an `is_animation_playing` guard (probed on 5.1: key insert → `Action`, selection click/transform-drag → not), so a selection click can no longer silently merge objects into the active shot; `frame_change_post` skips render jobs (`bpy.app.is_job_running("RENDER")` + scene-identity check — no Qt calls off the GUI thread); re-init tears down the prior controller's handlers/listeners (`ui._sequencer_controller` stash) instead of leaking them until `ui.destroyed`; easing-interpolation keys degrade to a straight preview segment (their handles aren't evaluation control points); `extract_attributes` resolves via `bpy.data.objects`. The false "Blender auto-removes a raising handler" rationale in two docstrings is corrected (it doesn't — it prints the traceback; the try-wraps stay). **View-mode option ported** (`_setup_shot_nav` now mirrors mayatk: Current→Adjacent→All cycle + "+" new-shot + refresh options — `_visible_shots`/`_set_view_mode` were fully built but unreachable, while the help text advertised them); the *Show Internal Holds* dead state is deleted, not ported — it rides the ledgered no-hold-sub-splitting divergence. `test_shot_sequencer.py` 65/65 (+6).
  - **Tube-rig b004 routes through the end control** (`tube_rig.py`): `constrain_end_with_falloff` auto-resolves the end's hooked control Empty (new `_end_control` — Spline IK constraint → driver curve → hook `vertex_indices`, scene-derived like mayatk's) and `CHILD_OF`-binds it to the anchor, so the whole end assembly follows; the `control=` parameter was dead (never passed) and on a spline rig only the falloff-weighted skin dragged while the IK curve stayed put. `RigUtils.apply_falloff_weights` rescales ONLY deform-bone groups (Maya `skinPercent` touches skinCluster influences only — it previously scaled every vertex group, corrupting cloth-pin/selection-set data in the radius). `test_tube_rig.py` 42/42 (+4: spline-rig CHILD_OF routing + non-deform-group guard).
  - **Suite/infra**: `test_blender_ui_handler.py`'s PANELS allowlist gains the three shots panels (the "no spurious panels" check was RED — 186/187; now 190/190; the sweep-count claims quoted in the entries below are superseded by the totals here); `Run-Tests.ps1` now also aggregates the `*_slot_check.py` harnesses (they emit the same sentinel but never ran in CI); `pyproject.toml` floors `pythontk>=0.8.85` (this changeset imports `Weights`/`TaskFactory`/`process_stream`/`engines.shots` — absent from 0.8.84); scene-exporter `_NEEDS_SHOTS` tooltip states the true remaining gap (the FBX-take projection, not "Shots port unstarted"); `reapply_object`'s one-sided public name is ledgered in `_shot_manifest.py`'s divergence list; stale "later phases" package docstrings and a wrong controller-class reference corrected.

- **2026-07-12 — Curtain drape engine vendored in-package: `edit_utils/_curtain_drape.py`.** `CurtainDrape` moved down from pythontk, which keeps only the general primitives it composes — the new `ptk.RailSurface` rail→(u,v)-grid primitive plus `Polyline`/`MathUtils`/`BandLimitedNoise` (a single tool's domain math isn't a pythontk engine; see pythontk's CHANGELOG same date). `create_curtain`/`CurtainSlots` now build from the local class — same parameters, same drape, no behavior change. Code-identical twin of `mayatk.edit_utils._curtain_drape`, drift-guarded by extapps' `test_vendor_sync.py` (`TestCurtainEngineDccSync`); the drape math itself is pinned once in mayatk's `test_curtain_drape.py` (12) — one test home per vendored engine, like the Marmoset/Substance copies. **Verified** in a fresh headless Blender 5.1: `test_curtain.py` 25/25 (identical 1921-vert build through the vendored twin, drift 0.00e+00, rig checks green).

- **2026-07-12 — `TaskFactory` + blendshape `Weights` de-vendored to pythontk; Substance connection composes the shared stream primitives.** The scene-exporter `TaskManager` now subclasses `pythontk.TaskFactory` (shared reflection-based task/check pipeline, formerly vendored). Blendshape `creator`/`slots` use `pythontk.Weights` (morph weight math, formerly `blendshape_animator.weights`). `substance_bridge/connection.py` sheds its bundled stream machinery — `OutputStream`/`ProcessReader`/`LogTailer` now come from `pythontk.core_utils.process_stream` (the app-agnostic mechanism); the Painter-specific `SubstanceConnection` shell + `substance_rpc` client + `templates/` and the whole Marmoset engine stay vendored **by design** (app-specific code fails pythontk's app-agnostic charter), kept code-identical with mayatk's copies by extapps' `test_vendor_sync.py` guard. Verified in a fresh Blender: `TaskManager` subclasses the shared `TaskFactory`; `Weights` resolves to pythontk.

- **2026-07-11 — Shot Sequencer PANEL ported (Blender) — the Shots trio is complete.** `anim_utils/shots/shot_sequencer/` gains the full NLE-style timeline panel over the shared `uitk` `SequencerWidget`. **Engine (`ShotSequencer`) expanded** with the surface the panel drives — accessors (`shots`/`sorted_shots`/`markers`/`hidden_objects`/`is`+`set_object_hidden`/`shot_by_id`/`define_shot`), key-motion (`move_object_keys`/`move_stepped_keys`/`move_object_in_shot`), key-scaling (`scale_object_keys`/`resize_object`/`set_shot_duration`/`resize_shot`), `_find_keyed_transforms`, `collect_object_segments`, and `reconcile_all_shots` (a no-op in Blender: flat unique names never go stale like Maya DAG paths). Blender needs none of Maya's cut-and-recreate key dance (`keyframe(timeChange=)` won't slide past an occupied frame) — every move is a direct `keyframe_point.co[0]` write, so the ports are simpler. **Display-data layer** (`segment_collector.py`): `collect_segments`/`active_object_set` delegate to the engine; `extract_attributes`/`build_curve_preview` read Blender fcurves (a keyframe's handles ARE the bezier control points in frame/value space — simpler than Maya's angle+weight reconstruction). **Four controller mixins** ported (`marker_manager` verbatim — DCC-agnostic; `shot_nav` — selection + `scene.frame_start/end`; `gap_manager` — `undo_chunk` swap; `clip_motion` — fcurve key edits + `curves_for_attr`/`scale_attribute_keys`). **Controller + slots** (`shot_sequencer_slots.py`, `ShotSequencerController` + `ShotSequencerSlots`): Maya's OpenMaya undo/redo/time/keyframe callbacks become `bpy.app.handlers` (`undo_post`/`redo_post`/`frame_change_post`/`depsgraph_update_post`, the last debounced + all error-guarded so a raise can't auto-unregister them); `cmds.currentTime`→`scene.frame_current`; the widget bridge (`_sync_to_widget`→tracks/clips/decoration) + all 34 widget signals wire to the mixin/controller handlers; header menu built 1:1. Ledgered follow-ups: sequencer **audio-track display** + **move-to-shot** sequence grouping deferred (the object-animation timeline is fully wired; matches the engine's "no audio shifting" note). Verified: `test_shot_sequencer.py` **58/58** live (engine + display-data), `test_shot_sequencer_panel.py` **8/8** (venv — real discovery+compile, all 34 signals wired to real slots), parity sweep `shot_sequencer` **0 untriaged deltas**; full sweep PASSES (Shots trio out of open work).
